*       *   June 29, 2021 – Cygnus Cargo Spacecraft Departs ISS Ending Four-Month Mission – A Northrop Grumman Cygnus cargo spacecraft undocks from the International Space Station at 12:25 p.m. EDT, more than four months after it arrived in orbit. After undocking from the ISS, the spacecraft deploys five small cubesats as part of a secondary mission.

*       *   June 29, 2021 – Russia Launches Cargo Ship to ISS – At 7:27 p.m EDT, an uncrewed Progress MS-17 cargo ship launches atop a Soyuz rocket from Baikonur, Kazakhstan, headed for the International Space Station.

*       *   June 28, 2021 – Study Finds There is Not Enough Water Vapor in Venus’ Clouds to Support Life – A team of scientists from the US and Europe report that there is not enough water vapor in the Venusian atmosphere to support life.

*       *   June 25, 2021 – Astronauts Install Second New ISS Solar Array During Spacewalk – NASA astronaut Shane Kimbrough and French astronaut Thomas Pesquet install the second of the new solar arrays for the International Space Station. The spacewalk is the astronauts’ third in less than ten days.

*       *   June 25, 2021 – Russia Launches Pion-NKS Ocean Reconnaissance Satellite – At 3:50 p.m. EDT, Russia launches the Pion-NKS No.901 satellite atop a Soyuz-2-1b rocket from Plesetsk Cosmodrome. The satellite is the first in Russia’s next-generation ocean reconnaissance system.

*       *   June 25, 2021 – Virgin Galactic Becomes First Company to Receive Approval from FAA to Conduct Commercial Space Flights – Virgin Galactic receives FAA approval to begin taking customers into space, becoming the first spaceline to cross that hurdle.

*       *   June 24, 2021 – US Air Force Conducts Second Skyborg Flight Test – The US Air Force conducts a second flight test of the robot pilot known as Skyborg, which autonomously flies a General Atomics MQ-20 Avenger drone. The MQ-20 Avenger flight takes place at Edwards Air Force Base, California, over a period of about two hours and 30 minutes.

*       *   June 24, 2021 – Bell’s V-280 Valor Technology Demonstrator Retires from Flight – Bell announces that its future vertical lift technology demonstrator, the V-280 Valor tiltrotor, has finished its flying career. Bell will continue into the second phase of the project with technology and lessons learned from the first aircraft.

*       *   June 23, 2021 – Space Perspective Opens Ticket Sales For Space Balloon Rides – Space Perspective opens ticket sales for trips on the company’s space balloon. The tickets cost $125,000 and would allow customers to ride on the Neptune One capsule for a six-hour trip to the stratosphere.

*       *   June 21, 2021 – Ingenuity Makes Eighth Flight on Mars – NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter makes its eighth flight on Mars. During the flight, Ingenuity remains aloft for 77.4 seconds, flies 525 feet (160 meters), and lands about 440 feet (133.5 m) away from its companion, the Perseverance rover.

*       *   June 21, 2021 – Volocopter Conducts First Public Flight of 2X eVTOL – Volocopter flies its Volocopter 2X in public for the first time in France at the Paris Air Forum. The flight takes place at Le Bourget Airfield and lasts three minutes.

*       *   June 21, 2021 – EASA Certifies Textron Aviation’s Cessna Citation CJ4 Gen2 – Textron Aviation receives certification for the Cessna Citation CJ4 Gen2 from the European Union Aviation Safety Agency.

*       *   June 20, 2021 – Astronauts Install First in Series of New ISS Solar Arrays – NASA astronaut Shane Kimbrough and French astronaut Thomas Pesquet equip the International Space Station with the first in a series of powerful new solar panels during a six hour and 28 minute spacewalk.

*       *   June 18, 2021 – Space Perspective Completes First Uncrewed Test of Space Balloon Prototype – Space Perspective successfully completes its first unmanned test flight of a gigantic balloon that will eventually take humans to the edge of space. The company launches a prototype of its stratospheric balloon 20 miles over Florida at 5:23am ET, putting in on schedule for the first commercial flight in 2024.

*       *   June 18, 2021 – Boeing Completes First Test Flight of 737 MAX 10 – The Boeing Company successfully completes its first test flight of the 737 MAX 10, the largest version of Boeing’s 737 jet. The test flight occurs out of Renton Municipal Airport in King County, Washington.

*       *   June 18, 2021 – China Launches Several Yaogan-30 Satellites ‐ At 2:30 a.m., China launches a group of classified Yaogan-30 satellites and one commercial satellite on a Long March 2C rocket. The China Aerospace Science and Technology Corp. (CASC) confirms the success of the launch within an hour.

*       *   June 17, 2021 – Astronauts Board Chinese Space Station – Three Chinese astronauts board the Tianhe core module of the in-development Chinese space station. The three astronauts – Tang Hongbo, Nie Haisheng, Liu Boming – become the first people to board China’s outpost in orbit.

*       *   June 17, 2021 – SpaceX Launches GPS 3 Satellite for US Space Force – At 12:09 p.m. EDT,, SpaceX launches a US Space Force GPS 3 satellite atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station.

*       *   June 16, 2021 – Ryanair Takes Delivery of its First Boeing 737 MAX – Ryanair takes delivery of its first Boeing 737 MAX jet after a delay of more than two years.

*       *   June 16, 2021 – FAA to Require Additional Inspections for 737 MAX – The FAA issues a directive requiring airlines that fly the Boeing 737 MAX to conduct additional inspections for the plane’s automated flight control system.

*       *   June 16, 2021 – China Launches Crewed Mission to Space Station – At 9:22 p.m. EDT, China launches a Long March 2F rocket on a crewed mission to the Tianhe core module of the country’s Tiangong space station.

*       *   June 15, 2021 – FAA Temporarily Grounds All Southwest Airlines Flights Due to Computer Outage – Southwest Airlines cancels about 500 flights and delays hundreds of others after it was forced to temporarily halt operations over a computer issue.

*       *   June 15, 2021 – Brazil Signs Artemis Accords – NASA Administrator Bill Nelson announces that Brazil has signed the Artemis Accords, becoming the first South American nation to do so.

*       *   June 15, 2021 – Three NRO Payloads Launch Atop Minotaur 1 Rocket – At 9:35 a.m. EDT, a Northrop Grumman Minotaur 1 four-stage solid fuel rocket launches three classified National Reconnaissance Office payloads from the Mid-Atlantic Regional Spaceport at NASA’s Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia.

*       *   June 13, 2021 – US Space Force TacRL-2 Mission Launches Atop Pegasus XL Rocket – At 4:11 a.m. EDT, Northrop Grumman launches the US Space Force Tactically Responsive Launch-2 (TacRL-2) mission atop a Pegasus XL rocket. The payload is successfully deployed in its intended orbit, a Space Force spokesman confirms.

*       *   June 12, 2021 – Winner In Auction for Seat on Blue Origin Flight Bid $28 Million – A seat on a spaceship ride with billionaire Jeff Bezos goes for $28 million during a live auction, concluding a month-long bidding process for the sightseeing trip on the Blue Origin’s maiden voyage.

*       *   June 11, 2021 – NASA Finishes Assembly of SLS – NASA finishes assembling its massive Space Launch System (SLS) megarocket that will fly astronauts back to the moon over the coming decade.

*       *   June 11, 2021 – DoD Certifies 3D-Printed Aircraft Part – For the first time, the US Defense Department certifies as airworthy a 3D printed aircraft engine part – an F110 sump cover produced under the Air Force’s collaborative initiative with General Electric called Pacer Edge.

*       *   June 10, 2021 – Leonardo Delivers First Next-Gen TH-73A Training Helicopter to USN – Leonardo Helicopters delivers the first next-generation TH-73A training helicopter to the US Navy.

*       *   June 10, 2021 – Archer Unveils its First eVTOL – Archer Aviation unveils its Maker eVTOL. The debut of Archer’s first electric aircraft is staged at a hangar using XR technology to simulate a ride.

*       *   June 10, 2021 – ESA Announces New Mission to Explore Venus – The European Space Agency announces it will launch a Venus-orbiting spacecraft in the early 2030s, named EnVision.

*       *   June 9, 2021 – Electra Unveils First Commercial Product – Electra Aero unveils its first commercial product to serve regional air mobility markets. The aircraft is designed to carry up to seven passengers and a pilot as far as 500 miles while operating out of areas shorter than a soccer field, including rooftops and parking lots.

*       *   June 9, 2021 – FAA Stands Up Rulemaking Committee For Development Of BVLOS UAV Operations – The FAA announces the creation of a new aviation rulemaking committee to develop a regulatory path for beyond visual line of sight (BVLOS) drone flights.

*       *   June 8, 2021 – NASA Conducts Seventh Flight of Ingenuity on Mars – NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter completes its seventh flight on Mars. The helicopter flies 350 feet south from its take-off site.

*       *   June 7, 2021 – Juno Spacecraft Flies by Jupiter’s Moon Ganymede – NASA’s Juno spacecraft conducts the closest flyby of Ganymede – the largest moon of Jupiter – in 21 years. The spacecraft flies by Ganymede at an altitude of just 645 miles (1,000 kilometers) at 12:56 p.m. EDT.

*       *   June 6, 2021 – SpaceX Launches SiriusXM Satellite into Orbit – At 12:26 a.m. EDT, SpaceX launches a Falcon 9 rocket carrying a satellite built by Maxar for SiriusXM Satellite Radio from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station.

*       *   June 5, 2021 – SpaceX Dragon Cargo Ship Docks with ISS – A SpaceX Dragon Cargo spacecraft autonomously docks with the International Space Station at 5:09 a.m. EDT, approximately 40 hours after the Dragon’s launch on a Falcon 9 rocket June 3.

*       *   June 4, 2021 – US Navy Conducts First-Ever Aerial Refueling Between Crewed Aircraft, Uncrewed Tanker – The US Navy conducts its first-ever aerial refueling between a manned aircraft and an unmanned tanker, with a Boeing MQ-25 Stingray test vehicle performing its first midair tanking mission with a Navy F/A-18E-F Super Hornet.

*       *   June 3, 2021 – SpaceX Launches Cargo Dragon to ISS – A SpaceX Cargo Dragon spacecraft launches atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Kennedy Space Center at 1:29 p.m. EDT Thursday.

*       *   June 2, 2021 – Russian Cosmonauts Conduct Spacewalk To Decommission Pirs Docking Module &dnash; Russian cosmonauts Oleg Novitskiy and Pyotr Dubrov begin a spacewalk outside the International Space Station at 1:53 a.m. EDT, and spend just over 7 hours working to decommission the Pirs docking compartment.

*       *   June 2, 2021 – China Launches Fengyun-4B Weather Satellite – At 12:17 p.m. EST, China launches a Long March 3B rocket, carrying a Fengyun-4B weather satellite, from Xichang Satellite Launch Center.

*       *   June 2, 2021 – Joby Aviation Announces Partnerships for Development of Skyports for eVTOL Aircraft – Joby Aviation announces partnerships with REEF Technology and Neighborhood Property Group (NPG) for the latter two companies to build landing and takeoff sites for Joby’s eVTOL aircraft.

*       *   June 1, 2021 – NASA’s Perseverance Rover Begins Science Mission on Mars – NASA’s Perseverance rover commences its science mission on Mars, starting with a move away from its touchdown zone June 1.

*       *   May 30, 2021 – New Zealand Becomes 11th Nation to Sign Artemis Accords – New Zealand signs the Artemis Accords, becoming the 11th nation to join the US-led accords governing best practices for space exploration activities.

*       *   May 29, 2021 – China Docks Cargo Spacecraft with Space Station – China’s Tianzhou 2 cargo ship docks with the core module of the country’s space station, delivering fuel, crew supplies, extravehicular spacesuits, and other gear in preparation for the arrival of three astronauts in June.

*       *   May 28, 2021 – Soyuz Rocket Launches 36 OneWeb Satellites into Orbit – At 1:38 p.m. EDT, a Soyuz-2.1b rocket lifts off from Vostochny Cosmodrome in Russia, carrying 36 OneWeb satellites.

*       *   May 27, 2021 – South Korea Signs Artemis Accords – South Korea signs the Artemis Accords, becoming the 10th signatory to the pact that governs norms of behavior for those who want to participate in the NASA-led Artemis lunar exploration program.

*       *   May 26, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station at 2:59 p.m. EDT, deploying 60 Starlink satellites 64 minutes after liftoff.

*       *   May 25, 2021 – UAV Startup Flytrex Receives FAA Approval to Expand Grocery Delivery to Thousands of Homes – Flytrex announces it has received approval from the Federal Aviation Administration to expand backyard drone delivery to thousands of homes in North Carolina.

*       *   May 25, 2021 – Airbus Helicopters Completes First Conversion of Four-Blade H145 to Five-Blade – Airbus Helicopters announces that it has performed the first conversion of a four-blade H145 light twin to the new five-blade version.

*       *   May 22, 2021 – Ingenuity Suffers In-Flight Glitch, but is Able to Land Safely – NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter attempts its sixth flight from Mars. However, 54 seconds into the flight, Ingenuity suffers a glitch that interrupts the flow of images from its navigation camera to its onboard computer.

*       *   May 22, 2021 – China Deploys Zhurong Rover on Mars – China’s Zhurong rover drives down the ramp of its landing capsule and onto the surface of Mars, making China the first nation to orbit, land and deploy a land vehicle on its inaugural mission to the Red Planet.

*       *   May 22, 2021 –Virgin Galactic Completes Crewed Test Flight of SpaceShipTwo – Virgin Galactic’s SpaceShipTwo, named VSS Unity, makes its first flight to space in more than two years, taking off from Spaceport America in New Mexico at 10:34 a.m. Eastern, carried aloft by its WhiteKnightTwo aircraft. The plane releases VSS Unity at 11:26 a.m. Eastern, at which time the spaceplane fires its hybrid rocket motor for approximately 60 seconds. VSS Unity ascends to a peak altitude of 89.2 kilometers before gliding back to a runway landing at Spaceport America at 11:43 a.m. Eastern.

*       *   May 21, 2021 – Supersonic Jet Developer Aerion Corp. Ceases Operations – Aerion Corp., the supersonic-jet developer founded by Texas billionaire Robert Bass and backed by The Boeing Company, announces it’s ceasing operations after failing to secure enough money to start building its AS2 private jet.

*       *   May 20, 2021 – Space Force Opens Space Warfighting Operations R&D Laboratory – The US Space Force opens the Space Warfighting Operations R&D (SWORD) laboratory, dedicated to the research and development of space military technology.

*       *   May 19, 2021 – China Launches Long March 4B Carrying Oceanogrpahy Satellite – At 12:03 a.m. EDT, a Long March 4B rocket lifts off from Jiuquan launch center, carrying China’s Haiyang 2D oceanography satellite.

*       *   May 19, 2021 – China Releases Images of Zhurong Rover on Mars – China releases the first images taken by its Martian rover, Zhurong, on the red planet, state television reports.

*       *   May 18, 2021 – ULA Launches Space Force’s Missile-Warning Satellite Atop Atlas 5 – At 1:37 p.m. EDT, a United Launch Alliance Atlas 5 rocket launches from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station with the fifth Space Based Infrared System (SBIRS GEO-5) satellite as the mission’s primary payload. The rocket also carries two 12U cubesats, which are deployed approximately 16 minutes and 30 seconds after liftoff.

*       *   May 18, 2021 – US Navy Declares Initial Operational Capability for Aircraft Landing System – The US Navy announces that Raytheon Technologies’ Joint Precision Approach and Landing System (JPALS) achieves initial operational capability (IOC) on May 4 and is now ready to facilitate aircraft landings on naval ships.

*       *   May 17, 2021 – NASA Names Fourth Astronaut to Crew-3 Mission – NASA announces it has assigned another astronaut to the next commercial crew mission to the International Space Station, scheduled for launch no earlier than October 23. Kayla Barron joins NASA astronauts Raja Chari and om Mashburn, and ESA astronaut Matthias Maurer.

*       *   May 16, 2021 – NASA Launches Sounding Rocket from Wallops – NASA launches its Black Brant XII sounding rocket, carrying the KiNETic-scale energy and momentum transport eXperiment (KiNET-X) payload, from Wallops Flight Facility at 8:36 p.m. EDT.

*       *   May 15, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 52 Starlink Satellites, Two Rideshare Payloads – At 6:56 p.m. EDT, a Falcon 9 rocket lifts off from Kennedy Space Center, carrying 52 Starlink satellites and two rideshare payloads: a synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imaging satellite from Capella Space and Tyvak Nano-Satellite Systems’ Tyvak-0130 satellite.

*       *   May 15, 2021 – Rocket Lab’s Electron Rocket Suffers Malfunction During Launch – A Rocket Lab Electron launcher malfunctions about two-and-a-half minutes after liftoff from New Zealand, destroying two BlackSky Earth-imaging satellites.

*       *   May 14, 2021 – Vandenberg Air Force Base Renamed Space Force Base – Vandenberg Air Force Base is renamed a US Space Force Base. Personnel assigned to the Air Force Space Command are reassigned to the Space Force, ending its Air Force lineage.

*       *   May 14, 2021 – China Lands Rover On Mars – China’s Zhurong Mars rover descends from Tianwen-1 touching down on the red planet, official news agencies confirm.

*       *   May 12, 2021 – MHI Conducts First Flight of XSH-60L Military Helicopter – Mitsubishi Heavy Industries conducts the first flight of the XSH-60L military helicopter, performing a 30-minute hover test from the company’s Komaki-Minami facility at Nagoya Airport.

*       *   May 11, 2021 – Space Force Renames 45th Space Wing to Space Launch Delta 45 – The US Space Force officially renames the 45th Space Wing that oversees Florida’s launch ranges Space Launch Delta 45.

*       *   May 10, 2021 – OSIRIS-REx Begins Trip Back to Earth – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft begins its trip back to Earth after its sample collection mission on asteroid Bennu. The trip back to Earth is expected to take about two years.

*       *   May 9, 2021 – SpaceX Flies Falcon 9 Booster for 10th Time – At 2:42 a.m. EDT, SpaceX launches 60 Starlink satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station. The Falcon 9 first stage used in the mission makes its record 10th flight.

*       *   May 8, 2021 – Debris from China’s Long March 5B Reenters Earth’s Atmosphere – The core stage of China’s Long March 5B rocket reenters Earth’s atmosphere, reportedly landing in the Indian Ocean. The US Space Command confirms the re-entry of the rocket over the Arabian Peninsula, but states it is unknown if the debris impacts land or water.

*       *   May 7, 2021 – NASA Flies Ingenuity for Fifth Time – NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter makes its fifth flight on Mars. Ingenuity retraces the course of its previous flight, heading south for 423 feet at an altitude of 16 feet, before stopping and climbing higher, to 33 feet, to take some pictures of the area. It then sets down, 108 seconds after take-off.

*       *   May 7, 2021 – F-35 Becomes US Air Force’s Second-Largest Fighter Jet Fleet – The US Air Force’s F-35 Joint Strike Fighter fleet officially surpasses the number of F-15 Eagle jets and A-10 Thunderbolt II attack aircraft, becoming the second-largest fighter jet fleet in its aircraft inventory, the service’s top general announces.

*       *   May 6, 2021 – China Launches Four Satellites atop Long March 2C – At 2:11 p.m. EDT Thursday, China launches a Long March 2C rocket from its Xichang facility carrying three military satellites and a civilian satellite.

*       *   May 5, 2021 – BETA Technologies’ ALIA eVTOL Receives Airworthiness Approval from US Air Force – BETA Technology’s ALIA eVTOL receives the first airworthiness approval for manned flight in an electric aircraft from the U.S. Air Force, the Air Force announces.

*       *   May 5, 2021 – SpaceX Launches and Lands Starship SN15 Prototype – SpaceX’s Starship SN15 prototype lifts off from SpaceX’s Starbase test site at 6:24 p.m EDT. The prototype climbs to about 6.2 miles into the sky, performs a number of maneuvers and comes back down for a safe touchdown at its designated landing pad six minutes after takeoff.

*       *   May 4, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites atop Falcon 9 Rocket – SpaceX launches 60 Starlink satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Kennedy Space Center at 3:01 p.m. EDT. The Falcon 9’s second stage releases the payload 64 minutes after liftoff while the first stage lands on a droneship in the Atlantic Ocean, completing its ninth flight.

*       *   May 2, 2021 – SpaceX Crew Dragon Splashes Down, Ending Crew-1 Mission – As part of the Crew-1 mission, the SpaceX Crew Dragon capsule undocks from the ISS at 8:35 p.m. EDT Saturday, and then executes a textbook return to Earth, dropping out of orbit, deploying four big parachutes and making a gentle splashdown south of Panama City, Florida, at 2:56 a.m.

*       *   April 30, 2021 – Ingenuity Makes Fourth Flight on Mars – At 10:49 a.m. EDT, NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter lifts off from Mars for the fourth time., covering 872 feet of ground and reaching a top speed of 8 mph during the 117-second flight.

*       *   April 29, 2021 – US Air Force Flies AI System Onboard Subsonic Autonomous UAV – The US Air Force flies an artificial intelligence system onboard a subsonic autonomous drone for the first time. According to an Air Force news release, the Skyborg autonomy core system, or ACS, is loaded into a Kratos UTAP-22 ‘Mako’ drone for a 130-minute flight test at Tyndall Air Force Base, Florida.

*       *   April 29, 2021 – Stratolaunch’s Roc Aircraft Conducts Second Test Flight – Stratolaunch’s Roc aircraft flies for its second time. The six-engine jet with the world’s longest wingspan takes off from Mojave Air and Space Port two years after its maiden flight.

*       *   April 28, 2021 – Vega Rocket Launches – At 9:50 p.m. EDT, Europe’s Vega rocket lifts off from Guiana Space Center, carrying a next-generation imaging satellite for Airbus Defence and Space.

*       *   April 28, 2021 – Apollo 11 Astronaut Collins Dies at 90 – Apollo 11 astronaut Michael Collins, who orbited the moon alone while Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin made their historic first steps on the lunar surface, dies at the age of 90.

*       *   April 28, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites – At 11:44 p.m. EDT, a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches 60 Starlink satellites from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station. Nine minutes after launch, the rocket’s first stage returns to Earth, touching down on SpaceX’s drone ship _Just Read the Instructions_, for its seventh successful landing.

*       *   April 28, 2021 – China Launches Core Module of its Space Station – At 11:23 p.m. EDT, China launches the Tianhe space station core module atop a Long March 5B rocket.

*       *   April 28, 2021 – FAA Orders Boeing to Address Issue Affecting Electrical Systems on Some 737 MAX Jets – The FAA orders The Boeing Company to fix bonding issues in the electrical systems of some of its 737 MAX planes that could lead to a loss of engine ice protection loss and critical functions on the flight deck.

*       *   April 27, 2021 – Wingcopter Unveils New eVTOL With Triple-Drop Delivery Capability – Wingcopter debuts a new all-electric vertical take-off and landing (eVTOL) fixed-wing drone capable of triple-drop deliveries. The Wingcopter 198 has a payload of 13 pounds and a range of 47 miles.

*       *   April 27, 2021 – FCC Approves SpaceX Plan to Operate Satellites in Lower Orbit – The FCC announces it has voted to approve a SpaceX plan to deploy some Starlink satellites at a lower earth orbit than initially planned as part of its effort to offer space-based broadband internet.

*       *   April 26, 2021 – SpaceX Test Fires Starship SN15 Prototype – At 5:57 p.m. EDT, SpaceX conducts the first static fire test of its Starship SN15 prototype.

*       *   April 26, 2021 – ULA Launches NRO Satellite – At 1:47 p.m. EDT, a National Reconnaissance Office satellite launches atop a United Launch Alliance Delta IV Heavy rocket from Vandenberg Air Force Base.

*       *   April 25, 2021 – OneWeb Satellites Launch atop Soyuz – an Arianespace Soyuz rocket launches 36 OneWeb satellites from Vostochny Cosmodrome in Russia at 6:14 p.m. EDT.

*       *   April 25, 2021 – Ingenuity Conducts Third Flight On Mars – NASA’s Ingenuity helicopter flies on Mars for the third time, traveling further and faster than its previous two flights.

*       *   April 24, 2021 – Crew-2 Docks with ISS – The SpaceX Crew Dragon capsule Endeavour docks with the International Space Station at 5:08 a.m. The Crew-2 crew becomes the first crew ever to be propelled into orbit by a rocket booster recycled from a previous spaceflight.

*       *   April 23, 2021 – Crew-2 Mission Lifts Off – A SpaceX Crew Dragon launches at 5:49 a.m. EDT from NASA’s Kennedy Space Center kicking off SpaceX’s Crew-2 mission, carrying four astronauts – NASA’s Shane Kimbrough and Megan McArthur, French astronaut Thomas Pesquet and Japanese spaceflyer Akihiko Hoshide – on a 24-hour flight to the International Space Station.

*       *   April 22, 2021 – Ingenuity Makes Second Flight on Mars – At 5:33 a.m. EDT, Ingenuity lifts off from the Jezero crater on Mars for its second flight on the Red Planet. The helicopter reaches a height of 16 feet and lasts 59.1 seconds.

*       *   April 21, 2021 – Kaman Conducts First Flight of its Uncrewed K-Max Titan Helicopter – Kaman flies its uncrewed K-Max Titan helicopter for the first time. The helicopter is controlled from the ground with a safety pilot on-board during its debut flight.

*       *   April 21, 2021 – Rule Allowing UAVs to Fly Over People and at Night Go into Effect – Final rules announced by the FAA in December 2020 officially take effect allowing for small drones to fly over people and at night.

*       *   April 20, 2021 – Perseverance’s MOXIE Instrument Converts Martian Carbon Dioxide Into Oxygen For First Time – NASA’s Perseverance rover uses its Mars Oxygen In-Situ Resource Utilization Experiment (MOXIE) instrument to generate oxygen from the thin, carbon dioxide-heavy Martian atmosphere for the first time, demonstrating technology that could help astronauts breathe as well as help propel the rockets that would get them back home to Earth.

*       *   April 20, 2021 – US Air Force Grounds Entire Fleet of B1-B Bombers – Commander of the US Air Force’s Global Strike Command Gen. Tim Ryan orders a safety stand-down for the service’s entire B1-B bomber fleet. The decision is made following a ground emergency on April 8 at Ellsworth Air Force Base, South Dakota.

*       *   April 19, 2021 – NASA Launches SHIELDS Mission Atop Brant IX Sounding Rocket – NASA’s Spatial Heterodyne Interferometric Emission Line Dynamics Spectrometer (SHIELDS) mission launches at 4:30 a.m. EDT, from the White Sands Missile Range in New Mexico.

*       *   April 19, 2021 – NASA Flies Ingenuity for First Time on Mars – NASA successfully flies its four-pound helicopter from the surface of Mars, the first powered flight of an aircraft on another planet. At approximately 3:30 a.m. EDT, Ingenuity lifts off the surface of the Red Planet, reaching an altitude of about 10-feet, where it hovers, turns and lands softly in an autonomous flight that lasts 30 seconds.

*       *   April 17, 2021 – NASA Astronaut, Two Russian Cosmonauts Return to Earth from ISS – NASA astronaut Kate Rubins and Russian cosmonauts Sergey Ryzhikov and Sergey Kud-Sverchkov return to Earth from their mission aboard the International Space Station. The crew lands in Kazakhstan at 12:55 a.m. aboard a Soyuz spacecraft, concluding their 185-day mission.

*       *   April 16, 2021 – NASA Chooses SpaceX for Contract Under Human Landing System Program – NASA chooses SpaceX’s Starship spacecraft as the winner of a $2.9 billion contract under the agency’s Human Landing System program.

*       *   April 15, 2021 – Gulfstream Begins Flying Final G700 in Test Program – Gulfstream conducts the sixth and final test aircraft in the company’s G700 program. The aircraft flies for more than three and a half hours, reaching an altitude of 48,000 feet and a top speed of Mach 0.935

*       *   April 15, 2021 – Northrop Grumman Conducts First Flight of Japan’s RQ-4B UAV – Northrop Grumman completes the first flight of Japan’s RQ-4B Global Hawk UAV from the company’s Palmdale, California facility.

*       *   April 15, 2021 – NASA Astronaut Walker Assumes Command of ISS – NASA astronaut Shannon Walker assumes command of the International Space Station.

*       *   April 15, 2021 – Xwing Completes First Autonomous Gate-to-Gate Cargo Flight – Xwing completes the first fully autonomous gate-to-gate demonstration of a commercial cargo flight, using a remotely-piloted Cessna Grand Caravan 208B utility plane.

*       *   April 13 & 15, 2021 – Sikorsky’s S-97 Raider Flies in Two Demonstrations for US Army – For the first time, Sikorsky flies its Future Attack Reconnaissance Aircraft prototype in two demonstrations for service leaders and soldiers at Redstone Arsenal in Huntsville, Alabama, performing high-speed passes, hovering, and showing off its maneuverability.

*       *   April 14, 2021 – Blue Origin Completes Suborbital Test Flight of New Shepard – Blue Origin launches its New Shepard vehicle from its Launch Site One in West Texas at 12:51 p.m. EDT. The mission, designated NS-15, tests procedures it will use for later crewed flights.

*       *   April 13, 2021 – US Air Force Completes HH-60W’s Development Test Program – The US Air Force finishes the HH-60W Combat Rescue Helicopter’s nearly two-year developmental test program marking a significant milestone for the service’s new fleet of personnel recovery aircraft.

*       *   April 12, 2021 – Northrop Grumman’s MEV-2 Servicer Docks with Intelsat Satellite – At 1:34 p.m EDT, Northrop Grumman’s MEV-2 satellite servicer docks with Intelsat’s in-orbit 10-02 spacecraft, **marking the first time** a satellite servicer docks with an in-service commercial satellite in geosynchronous orbit (GEO).

*       *   April 12, 2021 – NASA Unveils Photo of Martian Dunes from Odyssey Orbiter – NASA shares a photo of Mars in celebration of the 20th anniversary of the Odyssey orbiter, the longest working Mars spacecraft in history. The photo is composed of several images taken from December 2002 to November 2004 by the Mars Odyssey orbiter’s infrared camera.

*       *   April 9, 2021 – South Korea Unveils First KF-X Prototype – The first prototype of the Korea Aerospace Industries (KAI) KF-X fighter program is unveiled during a ceremony at KAI’s Sacheon production facility.

*       *   April 9, 2021 – Soyuz Launches Toward ISS Carrying NASA Astronaut, Two Russian Cosmonauts – At 3:42 a.m. EST, a Russian Soyuz 2.1a rocket launches from Baikonur Cosmodrome in Kazakhstan, carrying NASA astronaut Mark Vande Hei, and Russian cosmonauts Oleg Novitskiy and Pyotr Dubrov toward the International Space Station.

*       *   April 8, 2021 – China Launches Third Experimental Shiyan 6 Satellite – A Long March 4B rocket launches at 7:01 p.m. from the Taiyuan space base, carrying an experimental Shiyan 6 satellite. US military tracking data confirm the mission deploys its payload into an on-target orbit, and Chinese officials declare the launch a success.

*       *   April 8, 2021 – Tigerair Taiwan Receives First Airbus A320neo – Tigerair Taiwan takes delivery of its first of 15 Airbus A320neo aircraft, which is the first A320neo to be introduced in Taiwan.

*       *   April 7, 2021 – OSIRIS-REx Spends Almost Six Hours Imaging Asteroid Bennu in Flyby – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft conducts a flyover of asteroid Bennu, from which it had collected a sample in October 2020. NASA confirms that Osiris-Rex successfully completes the flyover and spends nearly six hours imaging the asteroid.

*       *   April 7, 2021 – Denmark Receives Its First F-35 from Lockheed Martin – The first of 27 F-35 Joint Strike Fighters destined for Denmark is presented to the country’s air force in a ceremony. The rollout event takes place at Lockheed Martin’s Fort Worth, Texas plant.

*       *   April 6, 2021 – UPS to Buy 10 eVTOL Aircraft from Beta, with Option for 150 More – United Parcel Service (UPS) agrees to buy 10 electric vertical takeoff and landing aircraft from Vermont-based startup Beta Technologies with an option to purchase up to 150. The first 10 aircraft are expected to begin arriving in 2024.

*       *   April 6, 2021 – NASA Conducts Second RS-25 Engine Hot-Fire Test of New Test Series – NASA conducts the second round of hot firing, from Stennis Space Center, in a new test series for the RS-25 engine, a component of the Space Launch System rocket.

*       *   April 6, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites into Orbit – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches at 12:34 p.m. EDT from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station carrying 60 Starlink satellites. The upper stage of the rocket deploys the satellites into orbit a little more than an hour later.

*       *   April 5, 2021 – NASA Shares First Weather Report from Mars’ Jezero Crater – NASA shares the first weather report from Jezero Crater on Mars. The Mars Environmental Dynamics Analyzer (MEDA) system aboard the Perseverance rover captures the [surrounding] temperatures for 30 minutes February 19 at around 10:25pm ET. The data shows that it is just below -4F on the surface when MEDA turns on, but drops to -14F 30 minutes later.

*       *   April 5, 2021 – SpaceX Reveals Cause of Starship SN11 Crash – SpaceX CEO Elon Musk tweets that the Starship SN11 prototype failed to land in the March 30 test flight, due to “a (relatively) small CH4 leak [that] led to fire on engine 2 & fried part of avionics, causing hard start attempting landing burn in CH4 turbopump. This is getting fixed 6 ways to Sunday.”

*       *   April 5, 2021 – Valkyrie UAV Deploys Smaller UAV from Inside its Internal Weapons Bay – The US Air Force announces that during the Kratos XQ-58A Valkyrie UAV’s sixth flight test at Yuma Proving Ground in Arizona, it opens its payload bay doors during flight for the first time and releases an ALTIUS-600, a small, tube-launched autonomous drone made by Area-I.

*       *   April 4, 2021 – SpaceX, OneWeb Satellites Come within 190 Feet of Each Other in Orbit – Two satellites, one from SpaceX and one from OneWeb, come within 190 feet of each other in space. The close encounter of the two satellites triggers several red alerts from the US Space Force’s 18th Space Control Squadron. In anticipation of the encounter, SpaceX inactivates its AI-powered collision avoidance system, allowing OneWeb to steer its satellite out of the way.

*       *   April 4, 2021 – Israel Unveils Advanced Intelligence Oron Aircraft – Israeli Air Force Commander Maj. Gen. Amikam Nurkin unveils the Oron aircraft, a nine-year secret project intended to create the most advanced intelligence aircraft in the world. A modified Gulfstream G550, the Oron combines the functions of airborne, ground and maritime surveillance, using both active sensors, and passive receivers for collecting signals intelligence.

*       *   April 3, 2021 – Ingenuity Touches Down on Mars – NASA’s Mars helicopter Ingenuity touches down on the surface of the Red Planet after being dropped by its mother ship, the Perseverance rover. Officials at NASA’s JPL relay in a tweet, “#MarsHelicopter touchdown confirmed! Its 293 million mile (471 million km) journey aboard @NASAPersevere ended with the final drop of 4 inches (10 cm) from the rover’s belly to the surface of Mars.”

*       *   March 31, 2021 – FAA Approves Design of Boeing 737-8200 – The FAA approves the design for the Boeing 737-8200, part of the Boeing 737 MAX series, a necessary step before the planemaker can begin delivering the airplanes to Ryanair.

*       *   March 30, 2021 – SpaceX Starship’s SN11 Spacecraft Explodes – SpaceX’s Starship SN11 prototype explodes, sending debris crashing to the ground. The spacecraft lifts off from its launchpad in South Texas around 9 a.m. Eastern time and cruises to an altitude of about six miles under the power of three engines, before descending back to the landing pad. But the onboard video stops 5 minutes and 49 seconds after liftoff, just as the vehicle reignites its Raptor engines for landing.

*       *   March 30, 2021 – China Launches Second Gaofen-12 Satellite – China launches the Gaofen-12 (02) satellite at 6:45 p.m. ET atop a Long March 4C rocket from Jiuquan, China.

*       *   March 30, 2021 – Virgin Galactic Unveils VSS Imagine Spacecraft – Virgin Galactic unveils the latest spacecraft addition to its fleet, VSS Imagine, with the spacecraft representing the first of its next-generation SpaceShip III class of vehicles.

*       *   March 28, 2021 – F-35B Completes Four-Week Sea Trials on Italian Aircraft Carrier – The F-35B Lightning II completes its four-week sea trials in the western Atlantic Ocean on the aircraft carrier ITS Cavour. The Sea Trials comprise more than 115 ski jump short take offs, 120 vertical landings, and two vertical take offs.

*       *   March 24, 2021 – Arianespace Launches Soyuz Rocket Carrying 36 OneWeb Satellites – Arianespace launches a Soyuz rocket carrying 36 OneWeb satellites from Vostochny Cosmodrome at 10:47 p.m. EDT.

*       *   March 24, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites – SpaceX launches 60 Starlink satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, at 4:28 a.m. EDT. The booster lands on the _Of Course I Still Love You_ drone ship stationed in the Atlantic Ocean approximately nine minutes after liftoff, completing that booster’s sixth landing.

*       *   March 22, 2021 – Rocket Lab Launches Rideshare Mission Atop Electron Rocket – Rocket Lab launches six smallsats atop an Electron rocket from Mahia Peninsula, New Zealand, at 6:30 p.m. EDT. The rocket deploys its kick stage eight and a half minutes after liftoff and, after a 40-minute coast, fires its Curie engine for nearly two minutes. Four minutes later, it deploys five payloads into orbit.

*       *   March 22, 2021 – Roscosmos Launches Soyuz 2.1a Rocket Carrying 38 Satellites – Roscosmos launches a Soyuz-2.1a rocket at 2:09 a.m. EDT from the Baikonur Cosmodrome. The rocket lifts off for three different polar orbits, with 38 satellites on board from customers in 18 countries.

*       *   March 21, 2021 – Year’s Closest Asteroid Flyby Makes Nearest Earth Approach – Astronomers observe a potentially hazardous asteroid as it comes the closest to Earth it has ever been – a mere 1.25 million miles from our planet’s atmosphere. The asteroid, called 2001 FO32, formed at the dawn of our solar system, and is the largest space rock expected to fly by Earth in 2021.

*       *   March 18, 2021 – Boeing, NASA Test Fires SLS Rocket – The Boeing Company and NASA test fire the enormous Space Launch System rocket for the second time, a full-duration eight-minute ‘hot fire’. At 4:37 p.m. EDT, the 21-story SLS core stage’s four RS-25 engines ignite at 120 millisecond intervals, quickly throttling up to a combined thrust of 1.6 million pounds of thrust.

*       *   March 17, 2021 – Final A380 Makes First Flight – The final A380 to be assembled takes off from Airbus’ Jean-Luc Lagardere plant at Toulouse-Blagnac Airport in southern France destined for the Airbus manufacturing plant at Hamburg-Finkenwerder Airport in Germany.

*       *   March 17, 2021 – NASA Releases Audio from Perseverance Drive on Mars – NASA’s Mars Perseverance rover records the sound of its six metallic wheels rolling across Mars, recorded during a 90-foot drive March 7. NASA releases two versions of the audio – one 90-second file edited and processed to filter out background noise, and another 16-minute clip with raw, unfiltered sound.

*       *   March 15, 2021 – Chang’e-5 Orbiter Reaches Lagrange Point 1 On Extended Mission – China’s Chang’e-5 orbiter successfully enters orbit around the Sun-Earth Lagrange point 1 as part of an extended mission, according to the China Aerospace and Technology Corp.

*       *   March 14, 2021 – SpaceX Breaks Record for Booster Reuse in Launch of 60 Starlink Satellites– A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches 60 Starlink satellites at 6:01 a.m EST from Kennedy Space Center. The launch is the third Falcon 9 flight in 10 days from two Florida launch pads, and sets a new record for SpaceX’s rocket reuse program as the Falcon 9 rocket’s first stage booster – designated B1051 – makes its ninth trip to space and back.

*       *   March 13, 2021 – NASA Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk Outside ISS – NASA astronauts Mike Hopkins and Victor Glover conduct a spacewalk outside the ISS to upgrade the lab’s communications and cooling systems.

*       *   March 11, 2021 – NASA’s Hubble Space Telescope Returns to Science Mode – NASA’s Hubble Space Telescope resumes observations after a software error places it in a protective safe mode several days earlier. The software error is traced to what an agency statement calls an ‘enhancement’ recently uploaded to the spacecraft.

*       *   March 11, 2021 – China Successfully Launches Long March 7A – At 12:51 p.m. EST Thursday, China launches its Long March 7A rocket from Wenchang Satellite Launch Center, sending a classified, experimental payload into geosynchronous transfer orbit.

*       *   March 10, 2021 – US Air Force Officially Accepts First Boeing F-15EX – The US Air Force officially accepts the first F-15EX fighter at The Boeing Company’s St. Louis production facility.

*       *   March 10, 2021 – Dassault Aviation Conducts First Test Flight of Falcon 6X – Dassault Aviation conducts the first flight of its Falcon 6X S/N 1, taking off from France’s Bordeaux-Mérignac Airport at 2:45 p.m. local time. The flight lasts approximately two hours and 30 minutes, and tests handling qualities, engine response, and key systems while climbing to 40,000 feet and reaching Mach 0.80.

*       *   March 9, 2021 – Russia, China Sign MoU to Collaborate on International Lunar Research Station– Russia and China sign a memorandum of understanding (MoU) to set up an international lunar research station.

*       *   March 5, 2021 – Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk – NASA astronaut Kate Rubins and Japan’s Soichi Noguchi conduct a spacewalk outside the ISS, completing assembly of two solar array support fixtures. The spacewalk lasts six hours and 56 minutes.

*       *   March 4, 2021 – Perseverance Rover Conducts First Test Drive on Mars – NASA’s Mars rover carries out its very first test drive on Mars, making a short 13-foot drive, performing a 150-degree left turn and then backing up about eight feet.

*       *   March 4, 2021– SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon9 rocket launches from Kennedy Space Center at 3:24 a.m. EST, carrying 60 Starlink satellites. The rocket drops its first stage booster about two-and-a-half minutes after liftoff before a single Merlin engine on the upper stage ignites to continue the flight into space, while the first stage successfully lands on the drone ship ‘Of Course I Still Love You’ positioned about 400 miles downrange from Cape Canaveral. The successful landing marks the 75th intact recovery of a Falcon rocket booster since December 2015.

*       *   March 3, 2021 – General Atomics Receives Second FAA Certificate for Avenger UAV – General Atomics Aeronautical Systems receives an experimental certificate from the FAA for a second Avenger Extended Range UAV. The certificate allows the aircraft to operate within the US National Airspace System.

*       *   March 3, 2021 – Space Vector Corp. Launches its First Sounding Rocket to Study Earth’s Ionosphere – Space Vector Corp. launches its first sounding rocket from NASA’s Wallops Flight Facility. The launch of the three-stage suborbital sounding rocket is to study the Earth’s ionosphere.

*       *   March 3, 2021– SpaceX Starship SN10 Explodes Approximately Eight Minutes After Landing – SpaceX’s Starship SN10 prototype launches from the company’s Boca Chica, Texas, test site at about 6:15 p.m and reaches a peak altitude of 10 kilometers before the vehicle performs a ‘belly flop’ maneuver to a horizontal orientation to descend back to its landing pad. The vehicle touches down on the pad softly, about six minutes and 20 seconds after liftoff and then explodes, approximately eight minutes after landing.

*       *   February 28, 2021 – Boeing 737 MAX Cleared for Service In Saudi Arabia – The Saudi civil aviation authority (GACA) announces that Boeing 737 Max planes will be allowed to return to service to and from the kingdom after meeting safety requirements.

*       *   February 28, 2021 – NASA Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk – NASA astronauts Kate Rubins and Victor Glover conduct a spacewalk outside the ISS, installing support frames for new, high-efficiency solar panels scheduled to arrive at the station in June.

*       *   February 28, 2021 – Indian Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle Launches Brazilian Satellite into Orbit – An Indian Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle places Brazil’s Amazonia 1 satellite into orbit to begin a mission monitoring deforestation in the Amazon rainforest, then releases 18 smaller spacecraft at a lower altitude, marking a successful start to India’s 2021 launch schedule.

*       *   February 28, 2021 – Russia Launches Satellite to Monitor Arctic Weather – At 1:55:01 a.m. EST, Russia launches a Soyuz-2.1b rocket carrying an Arktika-M 1 satellite from the Baikonur Cosmodrome. The satellite is the first in a new program to monitor Arctic weather systems and relay emergency communications.

*       *   February 27, 2021 – Boeing Completes First Flight of Loyal Wingman and Says it will use the Pilotless Jet as Basis for Skyborg – The Boeing Company conducts its first flight of the “Loyal Wingman,” a pilotless, fighter-like jet developed in Australia, as a Boeing test pilot monitors from a ground control station in South Australia.

*       *   February 25, 2021 – Australia Lifts Grounding of Boeing 737 MAX – Australia’s Civil Aviation Safety Authority lifts its grounding of the Boeing 737 MAX, becoming the first country in the Asia-Pacific region to clear the jet to fly.

*       *   February 25, 2021 – UK’s Royal Air Force Completes Last Surveillance Flight of Sentinel Surveillance Aircraft – The UK Royal Air Force’s Raytheon Sentinel surveillance fleet completes its final operation.

*       *   February 24, 2021 – China Launches Three Yaogan-31 Satellites – At 9:22 p.m. EST, China launches three Yaogan-31 satellites atop a Long March 4C rocket from Jiuquan Satellite Launch Center.

*       *   February 23, 2021 – FAA Orders Inspections for Engines After Boeing 777 Incidents – The FAA mandates fan blade inspections before some Boeing 777 jetliners are allowed to fly again.

*       *   February 22, 2021 – Cygnus Cargo Spacecraft Arrives at ISS – Northrop Grumman’s Cygnus NG-15 spacecraft arrives at the ISS at approximately 4:38 a.m. EST.

*       *   February 20, 2021 – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe Captures Photo of Venus During Close Flyby – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe completes its fourth swing past Venus capturing what NASA describes as a “fascinating view” of Venus.

*       *   February 20, 2021 – Northrop Grumman Launches Cargo Mission to ISS – Northrop Grumman’s Antares rocket launches a Cygnus NG-15 spacecraft carrying 3,810 kilograms of supplies and experiments to the International Space Station. The rocket launches from NASA’s Wallops Flight Facility at 12:36 p.m. EST, and Cygnus separates from the rocket’s upper stage nine minutes later.

*       *   February 19, 2021 – NATO Announces Initial Operating Capability of RQ-4D Fleet – NATO declares initial operating capability (IOC) for its fleet of RQ-4D Phoenix unmanned air vehicles (UAV).

*       *   February 18, 2021 – NASA’s Perseverance Lands on Mars – NASA’s Perseverance rover pulls off a thrilling seven-minute plunge through the Martian atmosphere to land on the surface of the red planet. The rover hits the top of the discernible Martian atmosphere at 3:48 p.m. EST and quickly decelerates in a blaze of atmospheric friction, its protective heat shield enduring temperatures as high as 2,700 degrees and a braking force 10 times the force of gravity on Earth.

*       *   February 17, 2021 – Air Force Begins Retiring B-1 Lancer Aircraft – The US Air Force begins the process of retiring its B-1 Lancer, as the ‘Bone’ bomber flies to the Air Force’s boneyard where divested aircraft are kept in storage. The Air Force plans to retire 17 of its 62 B-1 aircraft, and will cease service altogether in 2036.

*       *   February 17, 2021– UAE Lifts Grounding of Boeing 737 MAX – The United Arab Emirates civil aviation authority lifts its grounding of the Boeing 737 MAX, following similar action from the FAA and the European Union Aviation Safety Agency.

*       *   February 17, 2021 – TUI Fly Belgium Becomes First EU Airline to Resume 737 MAX Operations In EU – TUI Fly Belgium becomes the first EU carrier to resume service with the Boeing 737 Max since its March 2019 grounding with a flight from Brussels Airport to Alicante and Malaga, Spain.

*       *   February 15, 2021 – Russia Launches Unpiloted Cargo Ship to ISS – Russia’s Progress MS-16 unpiloted cargo ship launches successfully with a load of supplies for the International Space Station. The cargo craft is loaded two-and-a-half tons of supplies and equipment.

*       *   February 15, 2021 – SpaceX Successfully Launches Satellites, Misses Booster Landing – For the first time in a year, SpaceX misses the landing of its Falcon 9 first stage booster, which instead crashes in the ocean.

*       *   February 10, 2021 – ESA’s Solar Orbiter Captures Footage of Coronal Mass Ejection – The European Space Agency’s (ESA) Solar Orbiter spacecraft captures footage of a coronal mass ejection (CME) from the sun.

*       *   February 10, 2021 – China’s Tianwen-1 Successfully Enters Mars’ Orbit – China’s Tianwen-1 uncrewed spacecraft successfully enters Mars’ orbit, carrying out a 15-minute burn of its thrusters at 7:52 p.m. Beijing time (1152 GMT), slowing the spacecraft to a speed at which it can be captured by the pull of Mars’ gravity.

*       *   February 9, 2021 – UAE’s Hope Spacecraft Enters Mars’ Orbit – The United Arab Emirates’ Hope mission successfully enters Mars’ orbit after completing a 27-minute burn of its main thrusters, which slow the spacecraft down enough to enter an initial capture orbit around Mars, at 10:57 a.m. Signals from the spacecraft confirming a successful orbital insertion arrive 11 minutes later.

*       *   February 9, 2021 – Textron Unveils Citation CJ4 Gen2 Jet – Textron Aviation unveils the Citation CJ4 Gen2 jet, which includes an updated cabin of the company’s largest Cessna Citation CJ light jet.

*       *   February 8, 2021 – Kate Rubins Becomes First Astronaut Inside Operational Bishop Airlock – NASA astronaut Kate Rubins becomes the first astronaut to float inside the Bishop Airlock, the station’s first commercial airlock, which was sent to the ISS December 6 and is designed and operated by Nanoracks.

*       *   February 8, 2021 – Boeing Flies First KC-46A For Japan – The Boeing Company flies the first KC-46A tanker for the Japan Air Self-Defense Force, performing its maiden flight from Paine Field in Everett, Washington.

*       *   February 7, 2021 – Crew-1 Astronauts Set Record for Most Days on ISS by Crew from US-Launched Spacecraft – The four SpaceX Crew-1 astronauts, NASA astronauts Michael Hopkins, Shannon Walker, and Victor Glover, and Japanese astronaut Soichi Noguchi, who launch to the ISS on Nov. 15, 2020, eclipse the decades-long record for most consecutive days spent in space by a U.S.-launched spacecraft. The previous record is 84 days and is set in 1974 by the Skylab 4 crew.

*       *   February 4, 2021 – China Launches Satellite with Possible Military Purpose – At 10:36 a.m. EST, a Long March 3B rocket launches from Xichang space center, carrying what is believed to be a military-operated early warning station.

*       *   February 4, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites – SpaceX launches 60 Starlink satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral Space Force Station at 1:19 a.m. EST. Approximately nine minutes later, the rocket’s first stage returns to Earth, landing smoothly on SpaceX’s _Of Course I Still Love You_ droneship in the Atlantic Ocean, marking the fifth launch for this Falcon 9 first stage, which last flew just 27 days prior – the quickest turnaround between missions for any SpaceX booster.

*       *   February 2, 2021 – NASA Astronaut Millie Hughes-Fulford Dies – Trailblazing astronaut and scientist Millie Hughes-Fulford, the first female payload specialist to fly in space for NASA, dies following a long battle with cancer at the age of 75. Hughes-Fulford was selected by NASA for its astronaut program in 1983 and five years later, in June 1991, spent nine days in orbit on the shuttle Columbia, conducting experiments on the effect of space travel on humans as part of the agency’s first mission dedicated to biomedical studies, STS-40.

*       *   February 2, 2021 – Nanoracks’ Airlock Powers on for First Time Since Attachment to ISS – Nanoracks’ Bishop Airlock, which was attached to the ISS December 19, 2020, powers on for the first time. The airlock is the ISS’ first commercial airlock, and is the station’s first permanent, complex element to be owned and operated by a commercial company.

*       *   February 2, 2021 – SpaceX Conducts Starship SN9 Test; Rocket Explodes During Landing Attempt – SpaceX conducts a high-altitude test flight of its Starship SN9 prototype from Boca Chica, Texas. The prototype reaches a peak altitude of approximately 6 miles, then hovers momentarily in midair, shuts off its engines and executes a planned belly-flop maneuver to descend back toward Earth nose-down under aerodynamic control. The spacecraft explodes during its landing attempt after trying to reactivate two of its three Raptor thrusters, but one fails to ignite. The rocket then falls rapidly to the ground, exploding in a ball of flames, smoke and debris – 6 minutes and 26 seconds after launch.

*       *   February 2, 2021 – US Air Force’s New F-15EX Eagle Makes its First Flight – The first of the US Air Force’s new F-15EX Eagle fighter jets takes to the air. The new jet, described as the most advanced version of the F-15 ever built, makes its maiden flight from St. Louis, Missouri, departing at 1:53 p.m. local time.

*       *   February 1, 2021 – Rolls-Royce Demonstrates 100% Sustainable Aviation Fuel on G700 – Rolls-Royce successfully conducts its first tests on a business jet engine using 100 percent sustainable aviation fuel (SAF).

*       *   February 1, 2021 – NASA Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk, Complete Battery Upgrade Mission – NASA astronauts Mike Hopkins and Victor Glover conduct a spacewalk and complete a four-year effort to modernize the International Space Station’s power grid, installing one last battery.

*       *   February 2021 – Bell Demonstrates Autonomous Package Delivery in Residential Area – Bell recently demonstrates its Autonomous Pod Transport 70 (APT 70) cargo drone autonomously delivering a package over a 4mi preprogrammed route in north Texas. The demonstration takes place on an undisclosed date at the AllianceTexas Mobility Innovation Zone, a region that’s agreed to allow testing of and experimentation with innovative ground and air transportation vehicles.

*       *   January 31, 2021 – BluShift Aerospace Launches Prototype Rocket – BluShift Aerospace launches a 20-foot (6-meter) prototype rocket, hitting an altitude of a little more than 4,000 feet (1,219 meters) in a first run designed to test the rocket’s propulsion and control systems.

*       *   January 28, 2021 – 35th Anniversary of the Challenger Disaster – NASA holds a ceremony in front of Kennedy Space Center’s Space Mirror Memorial to remember the crew members who died on January 28, 1986. Flags fly at half-staff at NASA centers around the country, with small ceremonies also taking place at Johnson Space Center in Houston and Arlington National Cemetery in Virginia.

*       *   January 28, 2021 – China Launches Three Military Satellites Atop Long March 4C – China launches a Long March 4C rocket carrying three Yaogan 31-series military satellites. The rocket lifts off at 11:47 p.m. EST from the Jiuquan space base.

*       *   January 27, 2021 – NASA Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk to Upgrade European Module– NASA astronauts Mike Hopkins and Victor Glover conduct a spacewalk to make improvements on the ISS’ European module. The astronauts install a new antenna on Columbus, one of three high-tech labs at the orbiting outpost.

*       *   January 27, 2021 – EASA Clears Return of 737 MAX – The Boeing 737 MAX is approved to resume flights in Europe, following nearly two years of reviews after the aircraft was involved in two deadly crashes that saw the planes grounded worldwide.

*       *   January 24, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 143 Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – SpaceX launches 143 small satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket as part of the Transporter 1 mission. The mission, which launches at 10 a.m. EST from Cape Canaveral, includes 143 satellites atop the second stage which are the most ever launched by a single rocket, eclipsing the previous 104-satellite mark set by India’s Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle in February 2017.

*       *   January 20, 2021 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – At 8:02 a.m., SpaceX launches 60 Starlink satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from KSC’s Launch Complex 39A, and deploys the satellites 65 minutes after liftoff.

*       *   January 20, 2021 – Rocket Lab Launches Communications Satellite for OHB Group – At 2:26 a.m. EST, Rocket Lab launches a communications satellite for Germany-based OHB Group atop an Electron rocket.

*       *   January 19, 2021 – China Launches Comsat Atop Long March 3B – China launches its third Tiantong-1 mobile communications satellite on a Long March 3B rocket, which takes off from Xichang Satellite Launch Center at 11:25 a.m. EST.

*       *   January 17, 2021 – NASA Parker Solar Probe Performs Close Flyby of Sun – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe spacecraft makes its closest approach to the sun at 12:39 p.m. EST, at which time the spacecraft is about 8.4 million miles away from the sun’s surface, traveling at nearly 290,000 mph, according to the Johns Hopkins University Applied Physics Laboratory, which runs the spacecraft.

*       *   January 17, 2021 – Virgin Orbit’s LauncherOne Mission Successfully Reaches Orbit for First Time – Virgin Orbit completes its first successful test of its LauncherOne rocket. The rocket is dropped mid-air from the underside of a modified Boeing 747 nicknamed Cosmic Girl some 35,000 feet over the Pacific at 2:39 a.m. ET. The rocket then ignites its NewtonThree engine and propels itself out of Earth’s atmosphere, demonstrating its first successful trip to space.

*       *   January 17, 2021 – Greece Conducts Maiden Flight of Upgraded F-16V Fighter – The first of 84 Greek Lockheed Martin F-16 fighters to be upgraded to the F-16V Viper standard takes flight for the first time.

*       *   January 15, 2021 – Russia Exits Open Skies Treaty – The Russian government announces it is pulling out of a decades-old treaty that allows countries to make military reconnaissance flights over each other’s territory, escalating its growing military competition with the United States and Europe.

*       *   January 14, 2021 – Blue Origin Launches New Shepard Rocket, Capsule Test Flight – Blue Origin launches its New Shepard spacecraft at 12:17 p.m. propelling its unpiloted crew capsule into space. The rocket reaches a maximum velocity of 2,242 mph before releasing the crew capsule two minutes and 40 seconds after liftoff. The crew capsule soars to an altitude of 66.4 miles, before using parachutes to land 10 minutes and 15 seconds after launch.

*       *   January 13, 2021 – Air Force Says U.S. Space Command to be Headquartered in Alabama – The U.S. Air Force announces that the US Space Command will be headquartered in Huntsville, Alabama, after the state is selected over five others competing for the project, including Colorado, where Space Command is provisionally located.

*       *   January 13, 2021 – SpaceX Cargo Dragon Supply Craft Lands Off Coast of Florida – A SpaceX Cargo Dragon supply spacecraft lands off the coast of Florida at 8:26 p.m. EST, marking the first time a Dragon cargo mission lands near Florida, as previous cargo missions had ended with parachute-assisted splashdowns in the Pacific.

*       *   January 12, 2021 – SpaceX Cargo Dragon Capsule Undocks from ISS – At 9:05 a.m EST, a SpaceX Cargo Dragon capsule successfully conducts its first-ever autonomous undocking from the ISS.

*       *   January 11, 2021 – Boeing Delivers 84 Passenger Jets in 2020 – The Boeing Company posts deliveries of 84 passenger jets in 2020, a 90% decline from its all-time high in 2018, in addition to 157 deliveries for cargo and military customers.

*       *   January 8, 2021 – Airbus Delivers 566 Jets in 2020 – Airbus posts deliveries of 566 jets in 2020, making it the world’s largest planemaker. However, deliveries fall by 34% from a record posted a year earlier.

*       *   January 7, 2021 – SpaceX Launches Turksat 5A Comsat Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – SpaceX launches a Falcon 9 rocket carrying a communications satellite for Turkey-based Turksat at 9:15 p.m. EST from Space Launch Complex 40 at Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, marking SpaceX’s first launch of 2021. Thirty-three minutes after lift-off, the satellite separates from the Falcon 9’s upper stage and successfully enters into a geostationary transfer orbit.

*       *   January 6, 2021 – NASA Sets Cygnus Cargo Ship on Fire After Leaving ISS as Part of Experiment – The Spacecraft Fire Safety Experiment-V (Saffire-V), takes place after Northrop Grumman’s Cygnus NG-14 uncrewed cargo spacecraft departs the ISS in a study to help protect astronauts on long-term missions. Saffire-V lasts 26 hours, then the Cygnus spacecraft, filled with trash, burns up safely in Earth’s atmosphere as planned.

*   January 6, 2021 – New FAA Rules Open Door for Tests of Supersonic Jets – The FAA announces that it will ease the requirements for companies to receive permission to conduct supersonic test flights, which are currently prohibited over land.

2020

#### The History of Flight from Around the World

**2020**

* * *

*       *   December 31, 2020 – JetBlue Takes Delivery of First Airbus A220-300 – JetBlue Airways takes delivery of its first Airbus A220-300, which is built in Airbus’ Mobile, Alabama, facility.

*       *   December 29, 2020 – Boeing’s 737 Max Returns to US Airways – American Airlines flies a Boeing 737 Max with paying passengers from Miami to New York, the plane’s first commercial flight in U.S. skies since it was grounded following two deadly crashes.

*       *   December 29, 2020 – French Military Satellite Launches Atop Soyuz Rocket – A Soyuz ST-A rocket, carrying the Composante Spatiale Optique (CSO) 2 satellite, built by Airbus Space and Defence for the French military, launches from Guiana Space Center at 11:42 a.m. EST.

*       *   December 28, 2020 – FAA Issues UAV Rules on Remote ID, Flying Over People – The FAA issues a pair of rules, one that will require UAVs to be remotely identified by radio transmitters and another regulating drone flights over people.

*       *   December 28, 2020 – EPA Establishes Climate Standards for Jets – The Environmental Protection Agency establishes climate standards for jet aircraft. As a result, jet producers have until 2028 to comply with the standards, which restrict carbon-dioxide as well as nitrous-oxide emissions.

*       *   December 28, 2020 – Australia Announces its F-35 Reaches Initial Operational Capacity – Australia’s ministers for defense, Linda Reynolds, and defense industry, Melissa Price, jointly declare that the Royal Australian Air Force’s Lockheed Martin F-35 achieves initial operational capability, joining the U.S., Israel, Italy, Norway, South Korea, and the UK in reaching IOC for the type.

*       *   December 27, 2020 – Solar Orbiter Makes Closest Approach to Venus Yet – Solar Orbiter, a NASA and European Space Agency joint project, performs a flyby of Venus on its mission to capture the best-ever images of the sun. The flyby marks its closest approach yet, coming within 4,700 miles of Venus’ atmosphere.

*       *   December 27, 2020 – China Launches Yoga 33 Satellite Atop Long March 4C – China launches a Yaogan 33, believed to be a military surveillance satellite, atop a Long March 4C rocket at 10:44 a.m. EST.

*       *   December 21, 2020 – China’s Long March 8 Launches on its Maiden Flight – China’s Long March 8 lifts off at 11:37 p.m. Eastern from the coastal Wenchang launch site carrying five satellites into Sun-synchronous orbits.

*       *   December 19, 2020 – SpaceX Launches NRO Satellite in Company’s Final Launch of 2020 – SpaceX launches a satellite payload, dubbed NROL-108, for the National Reconnaissance Office (NRO) atop a Falcon 9 rocket from the Kennedy Space Center at 9 a.m EST. The Falcon 9’s first stage lands near Cape Canaveral Space Force Station approximately nine minutes after liftoff marking the second booster to land on the ground at the Cape, as opposed to a drone ship at sea, in 2020.

*       *   December 18, 2020 – Soyuz-2.1b Rocket Launches 36 OneWeb Satellites into Orbit – 36 OneWeb broadband relay stations launch atop a Russian Soyuz-2.1b rocket at 7:26 a.m. EST, bringing OneWeb’s orbital constellation to 110 satellites, and marking the first OneWeb satellites to launch since the company declared bankruptcy.

*       *   December 17, 2020 – Aeromexico Becomes Second Airline to Reintroduce Boeing 737 MAX – Aeromexico becomes the second airline to resume Boeing 737 Max operation since the model’s grounding with a flight from Mexico City to Cancun.

*       *   December 17, 2020 – India Launches ComSat Into Orbit – At 5:11 a.m. EST, India launches its CMS 1 communication satellite atop an Indian Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle (PSLV).

*       *   December 17, 2020 – Chang’e 5 Capsule Returns to Earth with Lunar Sample – China’s Chang’e 5 return capsule lands in the northern Chinese region of Inner Mongolia, the official Xinhua news agency reports, citing the China National Space Administration.

*       *   December 16, 2020 – Piper Aircraft Earns FAA Type Certification for Pilot 100 – Piper Aircraft earns FAA type certification for its Pilot 100.

*       *   December 15, 2020 – US Air Force Test Marks First-Known Use of AI on Military Aircraft – The US Air Force allows an AI algorithm to control sensor and navigation systems on a U-2 Dragon Lady spy plane in a training flight, marking what is believed to be the first known use of AI onboard a U.S. military aircraft.

*       *   December 15, 2020 – Astra Launches Second Orbital Test Flight, Reaches Space – Astra launches its second orbital test flight, Rocket 3.2, from the Pacific Spaceport Complex on Alaska’s Kodiak Island shortly before 4:00 p.m. EST.

*       *   December 15, 2020 – Russia Flies New Aircraft with Domestic Engines for First Time in Post-Soviet Era – Russia flies a new passenger airliner with domestically-built engines for the first time since the Soviet era. The medium-range MC-21 plane takes off from a Siberian airfield powered by Russian-built PD-14 turbo-fan engines.

*       *   December 15, 2020 – ATR Delivers its First-Ever Production Freighter Aircraft to Federal Express – ATR delivers its first-ever production freighter aircraft to launch customer Federal Express.” The delivery marks the first in FedEx’s contract with ATR that includes 30 ATR 72-600Fs, with an option for an additional 20.

*       *   December 15, 2020 – Rocket Lab Launches Synspective Satellite Atop Electron Rocket – A Rocket Lab Electron rocket, carrying a Synspective’s small radar observation satellite, launches from the Mahia Peninsula in New Zealand at 5:09 a.m. EST and deploys into sun-synchronous polar orbit approximately an hour after liftoff.

*       *   December 14, 2020 – JAXA Confirms Sample Is Inside Capsule Returned From Asteroid Ryugu – The Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) confirms the presence of black soil samples inside a capsule that the spacecraft Hayabusa2 brings back from asteroid Ryugu a week prior.

*       *   December 10, 2020 – ULA Launches NRO Payload Atop Delta IV Heavy Rocket – At 8:09 p.m. EST, a National Reconnaissance Office (NRO) satellite, NROL-44, launches atop a ULA Delta IV Heavy rocket, marking the sixth mission in 2020 for ULA and the 142nd successful launch for the company since its inception in 2002

*       *   December 9, 2020 – Air Force Gives First Safety Endorsement to eVTOL – The US Air Force gives a safety endorsement to Joby Aviation’s electric-powered vertical-takeoff-and-landing (eVTOL) vehicle marking the first safety endorsement the service has given to an eVTOL.

*       *   December 9, 2020 – SpaceX Conducts First High-Altitude Test Flight of Starship SN8 Prototype, Which Explodes Upon Landing– SpaceX conducts a test flight of its Starship SN8 prototype, lifting off at 5:45 p.m. EST from Boca Chica, Texas. At 6 minutes and 32 seconds after liftoff, Starship reignites its engines and reorients to the vertical to attempt a powered landing, but instead makes a hard landing 10 seconds later, exploding.

*       *   December 9, 2020 – Brazil Becomes First Airline to Reintroduce Boeing 737 MAX – Brazil-based Gol becomes the first airline to return the Max to service.

*       *   December 9, 2020 – JetBlue’s First A220 Completes Inaugural Test Flight – JetBlue’s first Airbus A220-300 completes its inaugural test flight from the Mobile Aeroplex at Brookley in Mobile, Alabama.

*       *   December 8, 2020 – Boeing Delivers First Post-Grounding 737 MAX to United Airlines– The Boeing Company delivers a 737 MAX to United Airlines, marking the first such delivery since US officials lift a nearly two-year grounding.

*       *   December 8, 2020 – Hayabusa2 Capsule With Asteroid Ryugu Sample Arrives in Japan– The Hayabusa2 capsule carrying samples from asteroid Ryugu arrives at a research facility in Sagamihara, Japan, near Tokyo, for curation and analysis.

*       *   December 7, 2020 – Chuck Yeager, First Pilot to Break Sound Barrier, Dies at Age 97– Charles E. ‘Chuck’ Yeager, a military test pilot who was the first person to fly faster than the speed of sound and live to tell about it, dies at the age of 97.

*       *   December 7, 2020 – Paris Airshow Cancelled Due to Pandemic– The Paris Airshow, set for June 2021, is cancelled for the first time since World War Two, due to the COVID-19 pandemic’s “unprecedented impact” on aerospace, according to organizers.

*       *   December 7, 2020 – SpaceX Cargo Dragon Docks with ISS– A SpaceX Cargo Dragon spacecraft autonomously docks with the ISS at 1:40 p.m. EST, as the station orbits about 268 miles above the southern Indian Ocean, near Australia.

*       *   December 6, 2020 – SpaceX Launches Next-Generation Cargo Dragon on Resupply Mission to ISS– At 11:17 a.m. EST, SpaceX launches a Dragon supply capsule to the ISS atop a Falcon 9 rocket, marking the 100th successful launch of a Falcon 9.

*       *   December 6, 2020 – China Launches Gaofen 14 Earth-Imaging Satellite– China launches an optical stereo mapping satellite, using an upgraded version of the country’s Long March 3B rocket with an extended payload shroud to deliver the Earth-imaging spacecraft into orbit 300 miles above Earth.

*       *   December 5, 2020 – Chang’e 5 Mission Completes Robotic Docking in Lunar Orbit– At 4:42 p.m. EST, China’s Chang’e 5 mission accomplishes the first robotic docking between two spacecraft orbiting the moon, when a lunar ascent spacecraft links up with an Earth return vehicle and transfers a container of moon rocks to bring home in mid-December.

*       *   December 5, 2020 – Japan’s Hayabusa2 Mission Returns to Earth with Sample of Asteroid Ryugu– Japan’s Hayabusa2 mission delivers samples taken from asteroid Ryugu to Earth Saturday, which could provide potential insights into the origin of life on Earth and evolution of the solar system.

*       *   December 3, 2020 – Chang’e 5 Ascent Vehicle Lifts Off from Moon with Lunar Samples– China’s Chang’e 5 ascent vehicle lifts off from the moon at 10:10 a.m. EST, carrying with it the first lunar samples since 1976. The spacecraft reaches lunar orbit six minutes after liftoff.

*       *   December 2, 2020 – Made In Space 3D-Prints Ceramic Turbine Part in Orbit – In the World’s first-ever demonstration of ceramic additive manufacturing in space, Made In Space builds its first ceramic part in orbit, 3D printing a single-piece turbine blisk (short for bladed disk) on board the International Space Station.

*       *   December 2, 2020 – Three Russian Comsats Launch Atop Soyuz Rocket– Three Russian Gonets communications satellites launch into orbit at 8:14 p.m. EST from the Plesetsk Cosmodrome, atop a Soyuz rocket, on the second successful Soyuz mission in less than 24 hours.

*       *   December 2, 2020 – Boeing 737 MAX Makes First Public Flight Since Grounding – The Boeing 737 MAX flies in its first public demonstration since its grounding, carrying members of the media from Dallas, Texas, to Tulsa, Oklahoma.

*       *   December 2, 2020 – Chang’e 5 Lands on Moon – China’s Chang’e 5 mission lands on the moon at 10:11 a.m. ET, marking the third successful uncrewed moon landing by China since 2013.

*       *   December 1, 2020 – UAE’s FalconEye 2 Satellite Launches Atop Soyuz Rocket – An Arianespace Soyuz rocket launches from Guiana Space Center at 8:33 p.m. ET, carrying a United Arab Emirates satellite, dubbed FalconEye 2.

*       *   November 30, 2020 – NASA Astronaut Rubins Harvests First Ever Radish Crop Grown On ISS – NASA astronaut Kate Rubins harvests the first ever radish crop grown on the International Space Station, which NASA describes as a “historic harvest.”

*       *   November 30, 2020 – Airbus’ Five-Bladed H145 Receives FAA Type Certification – Airbus’ new five-bladed H145 helicopter receives FAA type certification (TC), covering single-pilot instrument flight rules (IFR), single-engine operations (Cat.A/VTOL), and night vision goggle capability.

*       *   November 29, 2020 – Japan Launches Communications Satellite with Laser Technology – A Japanese communication satellite launches at 2:25 a.m. ET atop a Mitsubishi Heavy Industries H-IIA rocket. The satellite will make its way to geostationary orbit for a 10-year mission.

*       *   November 25, 2020 – Brazil Ends Boeing 737 MAX Grounding – Brazilian aviation regulator ANAC lifts the grounding of the Boeing 737 MAX, following U.S. clearance of the jets for flight.

*       *   November 24, 2020 – NASA Begins Assembling SLS – NASA announces that the Space Launch System’s first booster segment has been stacked on the mobile launcher at the Kennedy Space Center.

*       *   November 24, 2020 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – At 9:13 p.m. ET, SpaceX launches a Falcon 9 rocket carrying 60 Starlink satellites. Approximately nine minutes after launch, the Falcon 9’s first stage lands on the _Of Course I Still Love You_ droneship in the Atlantic Ocean, marking the seventh successful launch for this Falcon 9 first stage, a record for the company.

*       *   November 23, 2020 – China Launches Mission to Return Lunar Samples to Earth – At 3:30 p.m. ET, China launches its Chang’e 5 mission atop a Long March 5 heavy-lift launch vehicle from the Wenchang Satellite Launch Center, with a mission objective to collect and return approximately four pounds of rock and soil samples from the lunar surface.

*       *   November 21, 2020 – SpaceX Launches Sentinel-6 Michael Freilich Satellite Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – At 12:17 p.m. ET, SpaceX launches the Sentinel-6 Michael Freilich satellite atop a Falcon 9 rocket. The US-European satellite is designed to extend a decades-long measurement of global sea surface heights.

*       *   November 18, 2020 – Navy Flies Electric Hybrid Tiger UAV for 24 Hours Uninterrupted – The US Naval Research Laboratory flies a Hybrid Tiger UAV from the Aberdeen Proving Grounds in Maryland for more than 24 hours, its longest flight demonstration to date.

*       *   November 18, 2020 – FAA Lifts Grounding of Boeing 737 Max – The FAA lifts its grounding order for the Boeing 737 Max and publishes an airworthiness directive specifying needed design changes and new training requirements.

*       *   November 18, 2020 – Russian Astronauts Conduct Spacewalk to Prepare ISS for New Module – Russian astronauts Sergey Ryzhikov and Sergey Kud-Sverchkov conduct a spacewalk to prepare for the anticipated 2021 arrival of a long-delayed lab to replace Russia’s old Pirs spacewalking compartment.

*       *   November 18, 2020 – Taiwan Grounds F-16 Fleet After Plane Disappears During Training Mission – Taiwan’s air force grounds its F-16 fleet after losing a plane on a training mission. The U.S.-built F-16 vanishes shortly after taking off from the Hualien air base on the east coast on a routine training mission.

*       *   November 16, 2020 – SpaceX Dragon Capsule Docks at ISS – Four astronauts arrive at the International Space Station as the SpaceX Dragon capsule pulls up and docks around 11:00 p.m. ET, 262 miles above Idaho.

*       *   November 16, 2020 – Spanish and French Satellites Lost When Vega Rocket Malfunctions – The upper stage of a Vega rocket malfunctions minutes after liftoff from French Guiana, causing the loss of two satellites in the rocket’s second failure in its last three launches. The attempted launch takes place at the spaceport at Kourou, French Guiana, at 8:53 p.m. ET.

*       *   November 15, 2020 – SpaceX Falcon 9 Launches Dragon Capsule to International Space Station – At 7:27 p.m. ET, four astronauts launch into orbit atop a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket for a 27-hour voyage to the International Space Station in the first operational flight of a commercially developed Crew Dragon capsule.

*       *   November 13, 2020 – ULA Atlas 5 Rocket Launches NRO Satellite – At 5:32 p.m. ET, a National Reconnaissance Office satellite launches into orbit aboard a United Launch Alliance Atlas 5 rocket. The launch takes place from Space Launch Complex-41 at Cape Canaveral Air Force Station in Florida.

*       *   November 10, 2020 – SpaceX Wins Formal NASA Approval to Carry Astronauts to International Space Station – SpaceX wins formal NASA approval to carry astronauts to the International Space Station.
