*       *   November 7, 2020 – China-Based Galactic Energy Launches Small Satellite Into Orbit – Chinese rocket firm Galactic Energy sends a small satellite into orbit with the first launch of its Cere-1 launch vehicle, making Galactic Energy the second nominally private Chinese launch company to reach orbit.

*       *   November 7, 2020 – ISRO Successfully Launches 10 Satellites Into Orbit – The Indian Space Research Organization (ISRO) successfully launches 10 satellites to orbit, marking the 51st flight of India’s PSLV rocket.

*       *   November 6, 2020 – Japan Puts Bell Boeing MV-22B Osprey Into Service – The Japan Ground Self-Defense Force (JGSDF) puts its Bell Boeing MV-22B Osprey tiltrotor aircraft into service, making it the first international partner to operate the US-made platform.

*       *   November 6, 2020 – China Launches World’s First 6G Experimental Satellite – China reportedly successfully launches what it calls “the world’s first 6G experimental satellite” with 12 other satellites on a single rocket, according to Chinese state media.

*       *   November 5, 2020 – SpaceX Launches US Space Force GPS 3 Satellite Atop Falcon 9 Rocket

 At 6:24 p.m. EST, SpaceX launches a US Space Force satellite atop a Falcon 9 rocket. The satellite is the fourth in the GPS 3 constellation, which provides positioning, navigation and timing signals to military and civilian users.

*       *   November 4, 2020 – Navy’s Blue Angels Make Final Flight of F/A-18 Hornet Fighter – The US Navy’s Blue Angels conduct the final flight of its F/A-18 Hornet fighter, marking the end of the F/A-18 A/B/C/D platform’s 34 years as the Blue Angels’ aircraft, as the flight demonstration team transitions to F/A-18 E/F Super Hornets.

*       *   November 3, 2020 – Tu-160M2 Completes First Flight With New Turbofan Engines – A Russian Tu-160M2 Blackjack completes its first flight with new serial NK-32-02 turbofan engines.

*       *   October 27, 2020 – Flying AirCar Completes Test Flight – The AirCar, a flying sports car developed by KleinVision that can go from road vehicle to aircraft in just three minutes, flies 1,500 feet in the air over Slovakia as part of a test flight.

*       *   October 26, 2020 – China Launches Three Yaogan-20 Remote Sensing Satellites – The China Aerospace Science and Technology Corp. (CASC) confirms the launch of a seventh group of Yaogan-30 reconnaissance satellites. Three Yaogan satellites and one smaller commercial payload are launched on a Long March 2C rocket from LC-3 at the Xichang Satellite Launch Center.

*       *   October 26, 2020 – Studies Confirm Water On Lunar Surface; Moon Contains More Water Than Previously Thought – Two studies published in the journal Nature Astronomy confirm the presence of water on the lunar surface and suggest that there may be more water than previously estimated.

*       *   October 22, 2020 – Airbus Delivers First US-Made A220 –Airbus announces it has delivered its first US-produced A220, delivering an A220-300 to Delta Air Lines. The delivery comes 14 months after the company begins manufacturing the type at the Alabama site where it also produces A320s.

*       *   October 21, 2020 – Three ISS Crewmembers Return to Earth – A Soyuz MS16 capsule carrying NASA astronaut Chris Cassidy and Roscosmos cosmonauts Anatoly Ivanishin and Ivan Vagner return from the ISS, landing in Kazakhstan at 10:54 p.m. EDT. Cassidy, Ivanishin, and Vagner spend 196 days in orbit after arriving at the station April 9.

*       *   October 20, 2020 – NASA OSIRIS-REx Spacecraft Touches Down On Asteroid Bennu – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft touches down on asteroid Bennu. The Lockheed Martin-built spacecraft extends its 11-foot robotic arm toward a patch of gravel near Bennu’s north pole and grabs a sample of rocks, the space agency’s first handful of pristine asteroid rocks.

*       *   October 18, 2020 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Aboard Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches 60 more Starlink internet relay satellites into orbit at 8:26 a.m. EDT from the Kennedy Space Center.

*       *   October 13, 2020 – Soyuz MS-17 Reaches ISS In Record Time – Russian cosmonauts Sergey Ryzhikov and Sergey Kud-Sverchkov and NASA astronaut Dr. Kathleen Rubins arrive at the ISS approximately three hours after launching from the Baikonur Cosmodrome in Kazakhstan. The launch marks a milestone for a crewed Soyuz spacecraft, being the first to use a new ‘ultrafast’ rendezvous system with the ISS.

*       *   October 12, 2020 – Sikorsky Boeing SB-1 Defiant Reaches Speed Of 211 Kt – The Sikorsky Boeing SB-1 Defiant compound helicopter reaches 211kt (391km/h) using about two-thirds propeller torque and engine power during a straight and level flight.

*       *   October 11, 2020 – China Launches Gaofen-13 Satellite Atop Long March 3B – At 12:57 p.m. EDT, China launches the Gaofen-13 satellite atop a Long March 3B rocket from Xichang, China.

*       *   October 8, 2020 – EASA Certifies Airbus A330-900 – The European Union Aviation Safety Agency grants Airbus certification for its higher-maximum-takeoff-weight A330-900.

*       *   October 8, 2020 – British Airway Retires Its Last 747 – British Airways’ (BA) last jumbo jet bids farewell to London. BA’s predecessor, BOAC, introduces the 747 on the London-New York route in 1971, and at its peak has 57 747s.

*       *   October 7, 2020 – Boom Supersonic Unveils XB-1 Demonstrator – Boom Supersonic unveils its XB-1 demonstrator aircraft. The carbon fiber demonstrator plane is one-third the size of the planned 65-passenger Overture airliners the company intends to eventually build. With a cruising speed of Mach 2.2, the flight time from Washington, D.C., to Paris would be cut in half to four hours.

*       *   October 6, 2020 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop A Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket lifts off at 7:31 a.m. EDT from Kennedy Space Center carrying 60 Starlink satellites.

*       *   October 5, 2020 – Northrop Grumman’s Cygnus Supply Ship Docks With ISS – Northrop Grumman’s Cygnus supply ship reaches the ISS and is captured by the station’s robotic arm at 5:32 a.m. EDT.

*       *   October 2, 2020 – Northrop Grumman Launches Cargo Mission to ISS – At 9:16 p.m. EDT, Northrop Grumman launches its Cygnus capsule atop its Antares rocket from Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia. The Cygnus spacecraft hauls 7,624 lbs. (3,458 kilograms) of cargo for the Expedition 63/64 astronauts on the space station.

*       *   September 28, 2020 – Bell, Xwing Complete Uncrewed Flight Test Using Detect-And-Avoid System – Bell Textron demonstrates its Autonomous Pod Transport 70 (APT 70) vehicle as part of NASA’s Unmanned Aircraft Systems in the National Airspace System program. The flight is made using detect-and-avoid (DAA) hardware and software developed by autonomous technology specialist Xwing.

*       *   September 28, 2020 – GE Aviation’s GE9X Engine Receives FAA Certification – GE Aviation’s GE9X is approved by the FAA, the company announces. The largest commercial turbofan in the world, the GE9X powers the Boeing 777X, the new twinjet expected to gain certification sometime in 2022.

*       *   September 26, 2020 – China Launches Two Remote Sensing Satellites Into Orbit – China launches its Long March 4B rocket carrying satellites Huanjing-2A (HJ-2A) and HJ-2B at 11:23 PM EDT.

*       *   September 25, 2020 – RG Mk1 Protector UAV Achieves First Flight

 General Atomics’ RG Mk1 Protector drone, the fourth MQ-9B SkyGuardian air vehicle, achieves first flight.

*       *   September 24, 2020 – ZeroAvia Flies Demonstrator With New Hydrogen Propulsion System for First Time – ZeroAvia flies its Piper Malibu technology demonstrator for the first time with its new hydrogen propulsion system, including hydrogen fuel cells. The aircraft makes an eight-minute flight from the company’s research and development base at the UK’s Cranfield Airport, reaching an altitude of 1,000 feet.

*       *   September 24, 2020 – Brazilian Air Force’s Gripen E Flies In Brazilian Airspace for First Time – A Gripen E flies to its new home at Gavião Peixoto, Brazil. The flight marks the first time that the new fighter for the Força Aerea Brasileira (FAB, Brazilian air force) has flown in Brazilian airspace.

*       *   September 22, 2020 – NASA Changes ISS’ Position To Avoid Collision With Debris – NASA shifts the position of the ISS in order to avoid a potentially catastrophic encounter with debris that would have passed within less than a mile of the orbital laboratory.

*       *   September 19, 2020 – Australia Launches Dart Rocket Into Atmosphere for First Time – The Dart rocket is launched from Australia to the edge of space for the first time. The rocket is just 3.4m long and 34kg and will travel up to 100km into the atmosphere. The launch is conducted as part of the Royal Australian Air Force’s Plan Jericho.

*       *   September 15, 2020 – China Launches Nine Satellites Into Orbit From Ocean Platform – China launches a Long March 11 rocket from an ocean platform in the Yellow Sea sending nine satellites into orbit. The nine Jilin-1 Gaofen-03 (High Resolution-03) Earth Observation satellites will provide data for urban planning, disaster monitoring, and land resource surveys.

*       *   September 10, 2020 – MQ-9A Reaper Flies With Twice As Many Hellfire Missiles – A General Atomics MQ-9A Reaper UAV makes a sortie with double the number of Lockheed Martin AGM-114 Hellfire missiles, improving its combat persistence.

*       *   September 7, 2020 – China Launches Long March 4B Carrying Earth Observation Satellite; Fails With Return Of First Stage – At 1:57 a.m. EDT, China launches a Long March 4B rocket from the Taiyuan Satellite Launch Center, carrying the Gaofen 11 (02) Earth observation satellite, an optical observation satellite capable of returning high resolution images. Amateur footage posted on the Chinese social media site Weibo following the launch shows the first stage of the Long March 4B falling to Earth and exploding into a cloud of orange smoke.

*       *   September 7, 2020 – Wizz Air Abu Dhabi Receives Delivery of Its First Aircraft – An Airbus A321neo – Wizz Air Abu Dhabi, a joint venture between Hungary’s Wizz Air and Abu Dhabi-based holding company ADQ, celebrate the arrival of its first airplane – an Airbus A321neo – at Abu Dhabi International Airport.

*       *   September 6, 2020 – China Lands Experimental Reusable Spacecraft – China’s first reusable spacecraft lands Sunday after two days in orbit, the government announces.

*       *   September 4, 2020 – China Launches Reusable Spacecraft – China launches an experimental reusable spacecraft from the Jiuquan Satellite Launch Center. The Long March 2F launches at 1:23 a.m. EDT.

*       *   September 3, 2020 – SpaceX Launches 60 Starlink Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – At 8:46 a.m., a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket lifts off from Kennedy Space Center, carrying 60 Starlink satellites. The satellites are dispatched 15 minutes after liftoff.

*       *   September 2, 2020 – NASA, Northrop Grumman Test Fire SLS Booster – At 3:05 p.m., a full-scale flight support booster (FSB) test of NASA’s Space Launch System is conducted from Promontory, Utah.

*       *   September 2, 2020 – Vega Rocket Launches for First Time in Over a Year – At 9:51 p.m. EDT, Arianespace’s Vega rocket lifts off from Guiana Space Center carrying 53 satellites to orbit for 21 different customers from 13 countries. The launch is the first of a Vega rocket since July 10, 2019, and also marks the debut of Europe’s new Small Spacecraft Mission Service (SSMS) satellite dispenser.

*       *   August 31, 2020 – Amazon Receives FAA Approval to Deliver Packages With UAVs – Amazon receives approval from the FAA to use drones to deliver packages. The agency issues a Part 135 air carrier certificate to Amazon for its fleet of Prime Air drones.

*       *   August 30, 2020 – SpaceX Launches One Falcon 9 Rocket Sunday – At 7:18 p.m., SpaceX launches a SAOCOM-1B satellite for Argentina atop a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral. The launch marks the first rocket to launch a satellite into a polar orbit from Cape Canaveral in 60 years.

*       *   August 30, 2020 – Rocket Lab Launches First Electron Rocket in Two Months – At 11:05 p.m., Rocket Lab launches an Electron rocket from New Zealand, and deploys its payload, the Sequoia radar imaging satellite for Capella Space. The launch marks the first flight of the Electron rocket since a failed mission on July 4.

*       *   August 29, 2020 – OGO-1 Burns Up In Earth’s Atmosphere 64 Years After It Was Launched – The Orbiting Geophysics Observatory 1 (OGO-1), which NASA launched in September 1964 and retired in 1971, burns up in the Earth’s atmosphere, NASA confirms. The satellite was the first in a series of five missions to help scientists understand the magnetic environment around Earth.

*       *   August 28, 2020 – SkyDrive Completes Crewed eVTOL Flight – Japan-based SkyDrive completes a manned flight of its electrical vertical takeoff and landing machine, or ‘flying car’ that SkyDrive dubs the SD-03.

*       *   August 24, 2020 – Hill Helicopters Unveils New HX50 Helicopter – Hill Helicopters unveils the HX50, a new five-seat, light turbine single helicopter. Deliveries for the aircraft will begin in 2023.

*       *   August 22, 2020 – China Launches Three Payloads Atop Long March 2D Rocket – China launches a Gaofen-9 Earth observation satellite and two smaller payloads from the Jiuquan launch center at 10:27 p.m. EDT atop a Long March 2D rocket.

*       *   August 20, 2020 – Xwing Unveils System for Autonomous Flight – Automated flight startup Xwing introduces a system for fully autonomous flight. No fully autonomous, unmanned commercial aircraft operate in the US.

*       *   August 18, 2020 – Japan’s HTV-9 Leaves ISS as it Ends Final Mission – The Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency’s (JAXA) ninth H-II Transfer Vehicle, or HTV-9, is released from the space station’s robotic arm at 1:36 p.m. EDT in preparation for de-orbit, marking the end of 11 years of HTV missions.

*       *   August 18, 2020 – Three Planet SkySats, 58 SpaceX Starlink Satellites Launch Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket carrying 58 Starlink satellites and three Planet SkySat commercial imaging satellites launches at 10:31 a.m. from pad 40 at Cape Canaveral Air Force Station.

*       *   August 18, 2020 – SpaceChain Completes First Multisignature Blockchain Transaction From Space – SpaceChain UK Limited announces the successful execution of the first multisignature blockchain transaction in space.

*       *   August 17, 2020 – UAE’s Hope Spacecraft Successfully Completes First Course Correction Maneuver – The United Arab Emirates announce that its Hope spacecraft headed toward Mars completes its first course correction maneuver. The maneuver, or TCM1, requires firing up Hope’s engines for the first time.

*       *   August 16, 2020 – Asteroid Passes By Earth In Closest Approach Ever Recorded – A car-sized asteroid makes the closest-known approach to Earth without actually colliding with the planet. Asteroid 2020 QG flies past Earth at 12:08 a.m. EDT, getting as close as 1,830 miles away. It marks the closest asteroid flyby ever recorded in which the object actually survives.

*       *   August 15, 2020 – Arianespace Launches Ariane 5 Rocket Carrying Three Spacecraft From Guiana Space Center – Arianespace launches two communications satellites and a satellite servicer on an Ariane 5 rocket, completing the company’s first launch since the reopening of the Guiana Space Center in Kourou, French Guiana.

*       *   August 11, 2020 – Virgin Galactic Completes Successful Flight Test to Space With Founder Branson On Board – Virgin Galactic founder Richard Branson, along with five crewmembers, launch more than 50 miles above the New Mexico desert aboard his Virgin Galactic rocket plane and safely return in the vehicle’s first fully crewed test flight to space. SpaceShipTwo, attached beneath its twin-fuselaged mothership, WhiteKnightTwo, detaches from WhiteKnightTwo at approximately 11:15 a.m. and drops momentarily before its engines fire and the vehicle swoops upward. On board, the passengers experience up to three Gs of force from the burst of extreme acceleration.

*       *   August 8, 2020 – SpaceX Cargo Dragon Undocks From ISS – At 10:40 a.m. EDT, SpaceX’s CRS-22 Cargo Dragon spacecraft undocks from the ISS’s Harmony module, landing off the coast of Tallahassee, Florida, at 11:30 p.m. EDT.

*       *   August 7, 2020 – NASA Recharges Ingenuity On Way to Mars for First Time – NASA’s Ingenuity Mars Helicopter receives a checkout and recharge of its power system, one week into its near seven-month journey to Mars with the Perseverance rover. The recharge marks the first time the helicopter is powered up and its batteries are charged in the space environment.

*       *   August 7, 2020 – US Navy Conducts First Flight of Boeing EA-18G Using New Mid-Band Jammer Pod – The US Navy’s Next-Generation Jammer Mid-Band pod makes its first flight on the Boeing EA-18G Growler. The Safety of Flight test is conducted from Naval Air Station Patuxent River in Maryland.

*       *   August 7, 2020 – SpaceX Launches 57 Starlink Satellites, Two BlackSky Satellites Atop Falcon 9 Rocket – At 1:12 a.m. EDT, SpaceX launches 57 Starlink satellites and two BlackSky Global Earth-observation satellites atop a Falcon 9 rocket from NASA’s Kennedy Space Center. All 57 Starlink satellites are outfitted with a deployable visor designed to prevent sunlight from reflecting off the shiniest parts of the satellites.

*       *   August 6, 2020 – China Launches Two Satellites Atop Long March 2D Rocket – At 12:01 a.m. EDT, China launches its Gaofen 9 (04) and Q-SAT satellites atop a Long March 2D rocket.

*       *   August 5, 2020 – HH-60W Completes First Aerial Refueling – The HH-60W Jolly Green II completes its first aerial refueling at Florida’s Eglin Air Force Base. Air Force and Sikorsky pilots fly the aircraft at 110 knots to connect with an HC-130J tanker during the initial test, which lasts just under two hours.

*       *   August 2, 2020 – SpaceX Conducts “Hop” Test of Starship SN5 Prototype – SpaceX’s Starship SN5 prototype launches at 7:57 p.m. EDT from the company’s testing site in Boca Chica, Texas. The prototype ascends to a height of roughly 500 feet, then makes a controlled descent to a nearby landing pad.

*       *   August 2, 2020 – NASA Astronauts Return to Earth, Complete Demo-2 Mission – NASA astronauts Bob Behnken and Doug Hurley, aboard the SpaceX Crew Dragon capsule Endeavour, splash down off the coast of Pensacola, Florida, at 2:48 p.m. EDT, bringing an end to SpaceX’s historic Demo-2 mission to the ISS.

*       *   July 30, 2020 – NASA Launches Mars 2020 Mission – NASA’s Perseverance rover launches at 7:50 a.m. EDT atop a ULA Atlas V rocket from Space Launch Complex 41 at Cape Canaveral Air Force Station in Florida. The rocket leaves Earth’s orbit about an hour after the launch. If all goes as planned, the rover will descend to the Martian surface on Feb. 18, 2021.

*       *   July 28, 2020 – NASA Launches Mars 2020 Mission – NASA’s Perseverance rover launches at 7:50 a.m. EDT atop a ULA Atlas V rocket from Space Launch Complex 41 at Cape Canaveral Air Force Station in Florida. The rocket leaves Earth’s orbit about an hour after the launch. If all goes as planned, the rover will descend to the Martian surface on Feb. 18, 2021.

*       *   July 28, 2020 – NASA Announces Astronauts for SpaceX Crew-2 Mission in Spring 2021 – NASA and its international partners officially assign the astronauts to fly on SpaceX’s Crew-2 mission in spring of 2021. The astronauts in Crew-2 include: NASA astronauts Shane Kimbrough and Megan McArthur, who will serve as spacecraft commander and pilot, respectively, Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency astronaut Akihiko Hoshide, and ESA astronaut Thomas Pesquet, who will both serve as specialists for the mission.

*       *   July 24, 2020 – China Launches Three Payloads, Including Remote Sensing Satellite, Atop Long March 4B Rocket – China launches three satellites atop a Long March 4B rocket at 11:13 p.m EDT. The China Aerospace Science and Technology Corp. confirms launch success within the hour.

*       *   July 24, 2020 – Russia Launches Cargo Ship to ISS – Russia launches the uncrewed Progress MS-15 cargo ship to the ISS carrying about three tons of fuel, water, food, medicine and other supplies.

*       *   July 23, 2020 – China Launches Tianwen-1 Mission Toward Mars – China launches an unmanned probe to Mars in its first independent mission to another planet. China’s Long March 5 Y-4 rocket blasts off with the probe at 12:41 p.m. ET from Wenchang Space Launch Centre on the island of Hainan. The probe is expected to reach Mars in February 2021 where it will attempt to deploy a rover to explore the planet for 90 days.

*       *   July 21, 2020 – NASA Astronauts Behnken, Cassidy Conduct Final Spacewalk Before SpaceX Capsule Returns to Earth – NASA astronauts Bob Behnken and Chris Cassidy complete their final spacewalk outside the ISS before preparing for the SpaceX Dragon crew capsule’s return flight to Earth.

*       *   July 20, 2020 – SpaceX Launches Anasis-II Military Satellite For South Korea – SpaceX launches South Korea’s Anasis-II military satellite atop a Falcon 9 rocket at 5:30 p.m. EDT. The rocket deploys South Korea’s first dedicated military satellite into orbit a half-hour after launch from Cape Canaveral, helping fulfill an agreement between Lockheed Martin and the South Korean government.

*       *   July 19, 2020 – UAE’s Hope Mission Launches Toward Mars – The United Arab Emirates’ Mars mission launches atop a Mitsubishi Heavy Industries H-2A rocket at 5:58 p.m. EDT from the Tanegashima Space Center in Japan.

*       *   July 16, 2020 – NASA Astronauts Behnken, Cassidy Complete Third Spacewalk of Mission – NASA astronauts Bob Behnken and Chris Cassidy complete their part of a three-year power upgrade to the ISS, replacing six more outdated batteries with powerful new ones. The astronauts are able to complete the battery swaps in a single spacewalk rather than two.

*       *   July 16, 2020 – NASA, ESA Solar Orbiter Releases Closest Images of Sun Ever Taken – NASA and ESA release the closest pictures ever taken of the sun. The images are taken by the Solar Orbiter, an international collaboration between ESA and NASA

*       *   July 15, 2020 – NRO, US Space Force Launch Spy Satellites atop Minotaur IV Rocket – A Northrop Grumman Minotaur IV rocket launches at 9:46 a.m. from the Mid-Atlantic Regional Spaceport in Virginia, carrying the NROL-129 mission to orbit for the US National Reconnaissance Office (NRO) and the US Space Force.

*       *   July 13, 2020 – NASA Completes Assembly, Initial Systems Testing of James Webb Space Telescope – NASA concludes assembly and initial full-systems testing of the James Webb Space Telescope built by Northrop Grumman and Ball Aerospace.

*       *   July 10, 2020 – Astronomers Find Galactic Wall Hidden From Sight Behind Milky Way Galaxy – In a paper published July 10 in the Astrophysical Journal, astronomers say that they have located what they call the South Pole Wall – a hidden conglomeration of galaxies behind the Milky Way, in what they call the ‘zone of avoidance.’

*       *   July 10, 2020 – Air China Conducts Maiden Flight of Its Comac ARJ21 – Air China completes the maiden flight of its Comac ARJ21 regional jet via domestic routing from Beijing Capital airport to Xilinhot.

*       *   July 10, 2020 – First Launch of Kuaizhou-11 Fails, Loses Two Satellites – The attempted launch of a new Chinese Kuaizhou-11 commercial solid rocket ends in failure resulting in the loss of two satellites. The rocket lifts off at 12:17 a.m. EDT from Jiuquan Satellite Launch Center in China.

*       *   July 9, 2020 – Apstar-6D Satellite Launches Atop Long March 3B Rocket – The Apstar-6D satellite launches atop a Long March 3B rocket at 8:11 a.m. EDT on Thursday from China’s Xichang Satellite Launch Center. The high-throughput satellite is the first commercial success of China’s enhanced Dongfanghong-4 platform

*       *   July 4, 2020 – NASA’s TESS Space Telescope Finishes Primary Mission – NASA’s Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) space telescope finishes its primary mission, having discovered 66 confirmed alien planets and nearly 2,100 ‘candidates.’ NASA will continue to use TESS to find exoplanets in an extended mission that runs through September 2022.

*       *   July 4, 2020 – China Launches Shiyan-6, Gaofen Remote Sensing Satellites Within Three Days – China launches the classified Shiyan-6 (02) satellite into orbit, following launch of a new Gaofen Earth observation satellite two days earlier. The Shiyan-6, which is carried into orbit by a Long March 2D rocket, is an experimental remote sensing satellite which is launched from Jiuquan, China at 7:45 p.m. EST.

*       *   July 1, 2020 – NASA Astronauts Perform Second Spacewalk of Week – NASA astronauts Chris Cassidy and Bob Behnken complete their second spacewalk in under a week to replace old batteries outside the ISS. During the six-hour spacewalk, the astronauts install another new battery and route power and Ethernet cables outside the outpost.

*       *   July 1, 2020 – Boeing, FAA Finish 737 MAX Test Flights – The Boeing Company and the FAA complete the third and final certification test flight for the 737 MAX, a key milestone toward the plane’s return to service after its grounding in March 2019.

*       *   June 30, 2020 – SpaceX Launches US Space Force’s GPS 3 Satellite Into Orbit – At 4:10 p.m. EDT, a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket launches from Cape Canaveral, Florida, carrying a US Space Force GPS 3 satellite made by Lockheed Martin. The satellite is successfully deployed in a medium Earth orbit at an altitude of about 12,550 miles.

*       *   June 29, 2020 – Airbus Completes Two Years of ATTOL Flight Testing With Demonstration – Airbus completes two years of flight testing for its Autonomous Taxi, Take-Off, and Landing (ATTOL) project, which culminates in a fully automatic, vision-based flight of an A350-1000 widebody airliner. The flight is achieved through the use of on-board image recognition technology, which the manufacturer describes as a “world-first” in a June 29 press statement.

*       *   June 26, 2020 – NASA Astronauts Complete Spacewalk Outside ISS – NASA astronauts Chris Cassidy and Bob Behnken complete a spacewalk outside the ISS that lasts 6 hours and 7 minutes. The walk concludes at 1:39 p.m. EDT, after the astronauts successfully swap out three aging nickel-hydrogen batteries for two more efficient lithium-ion batteries in the first of four spacewalks to upgrade the ISS’ solar arrays.

*       *   June 25, 2020 – Virgin Galactic Completes Test Flight of SpaceShipTwo – Virgin Galactic conducts its second test flight of its SpaceShipTwo suborbital vehicle from Spaceport America in New Mexico, making it ready to resume powered flights of the spacecraft. During the test, the spacecraft reached speeds of Mach 0.85.

*       *   June 25, 2020 – Bombardier Global 5500 Enters Service – Bombardier announces that the Global 5500 aircraft has entered service after it is delivered to an unnamed customer.

*       *   June 24, 2020 – US Naval Air Systems Command Wraps Up Sea Trial of CH-53K – The US Navy announces US Naval Air Systems Command completed a two-week trial of the Sikorsky CH-53K helicopter from the USS Wasp assault ship stationed in the Atlantic Ocean. The CH-53K is scheduled to replace the US Marine Corps’ CH-53E Super Stallion as an air transport for troops and heavy equipment.

*       *   June 23, 2020 – China Launches Final Satellite for Beidou-3 Network – China successfully puts into orbit the final satellite of its Beidou navigation network, rival to the U.S.-owned GPS. The Beidou-3 satellite is the 35th and final satellite of the Chinese navigation system – an estimated $10 billion project.

*       *   June 22, 2020 – Virgin Galactic Signs Agreement With NASA to Offer Private Spaceflight Experiences – Virgin Galactic announces it has signed an agreement with NASA to support development of a program to train and potentially procure rides for private individuals seeking to fly to the International Space Station.

*       *   June 22, 2020 – Taiwan Conducts First Public Flight Test of AT-5 Trainer – Taiwan President Tsai Ing-wen oversees the first public test flight of a new locally designed and made advanced jet trainer.

*       *   June 17, 2020 – US Fighter Jets Intercept Russian Bombers Outside of Alaskan Airspace – US fighter jets intercept two formations of Russian military aircraft off the coast of Alaska for the second time in a week, North American Aerospace Defense Command [NORAD] announces in a June 17 statement. F-22 Raptors, backed by KC-135 aerial refueling tankers and an E-3 early warning aircraft, intercept the Russian bombers in the Alaskan Air Defense Identification Zone on the evening of June 16, according to NORAD.

*       *   June 17, 2020 – China Launches Long March 2D Rocket Carrying Three Payloads – A Chinese Long March 2D rocket launches from Jiuquan space base at 3:19 a.m. EDT (3:19 p.m. local time), carrying a Gaofen Earth-imaging satellite and two secondary payloads.

*       *   June 17, 2020 – Gulfstream To End Production of G550 Aircraft – Gulfstream announces it is halting production of the G550, ending a 17-year run for the long-range business jet.

*       *   June 16, 2020 – DoD Publishes Defense Space Strategy – The DoD releases its Defense Space Strategy, designed to maintain U.S. military superiority in space versus growing counter-space efforts in Russia and China.

*       *   June 15, 2020 – Japan Halts Deployment of US Missile Defense System – Japan’s Defense Ministry announces that it has decided to stop plans to deploy two land-based U.S. missile defense systems. The decision to stop the deployment of the Aegis Ashore systems comes after it was found that the safety of one of the two planned host communities could not be ensured without a hardware redesign that would be too time consuming and costly.

*       *   June 15, 2020 – ESA’s Solar Orbiter Comes Within 48 Million Miles of Sun – The ESA’s Solar Orbiter makes its first close pass of the sun, getting as close as 48 million miles from the sun’s surface. The probe makes its closest approach at around 3.35 a.m Eastern standard time.

*       *   June 14, 2020 – NASA’s New Horizons Spacecraft Sends Back Images of Parallax Effect – NASA announces that its New Horizons spacecraft, using its unique vantage point 4.3 billion miles from Earth, captures images of nearby stars in which the stars appear to be in different positions than where they’re seen from Earth. The images represent the first time the ‘parallax effect’ has been captured using a spacecraft.

*       *   June 13, 2020 – Skyrora Launches Nano Rocket From Shetland Islands – Skyrora launches its Nano rocket into suborbital from the Shetland Islands, Scotland, marking the first launch conducted from the site.

*       *   June 13, 2020 – SpaceX Launches Starlink Satellites, SkySats Aboard Falcon 9 Rocket – A SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket, carrying 58 Starlink satellites and three of Planet’s SkySats, launches at 5:21 a.m. EDT from pad 40 of Cape Canaveral’s Air Force Station. SpaceX’s previous Falcon 9 launch takes off from the same location less than 10 days prior, the fastest turnaround between Falcon 9 flights from the same launch pad in the company’s history.

*       *   June 13, 2020 – Rocket Lab Launches Electron Rocket With Five Payloads – A Rocket Lab Electron rocket successfully launches a set of payloads for the National Reconnaissance Office and two universities on a mission delayed two and a half months by the coronavirus pandemic.

*       *   June 10, 2020 – China Launches Ocean Observation Satellite – At 4:31 p.m. EST, China launches its Haiyang-1D ocean observation satellite from the Taiyuan Satellite Launch Center. The success of the launch is confirmed by state-owned China Aerospace Science and Technology Corp. within 30 minutes of the launch.

*       *   June 9, 2020 – Sikorsky-Boeing SB-1 Helicopter Reaches Speeds of More Than 200kt – in a test flight test, Sikorsky-Boeing’s SB-1 Defiant co-axial helicopter passes 200kt for the first time while flying at Sikorsky’s West Palm Beach Development Flight Test Center in Florida. The helicopter tops out at 205kt (380 km/hr).

*       *   June 7, 2020 – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe Comes Within 12 Million Miles of Sun – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe makes its fifth flyby of the sun at 4:23 a.m. EDT, Sunday, June 7.

*       *   June 2, 2020 – Delta Retires Last McDonnell Douglas Aircraft – Delta Air Lines makes the last flight of its McDonnell Douglas aircraft. Delta is the last U.S. airline to operate the planes after American Airlines retires its MD-80 fleet in September 2019.

*       *   May 30, 2020 – NASA and SpaceX Launch New Era of Spaceflight – For the first time in history, NASA astronauts launch from U.S. soil in a commercially built and operated American spacecraft on its way to the ISS. The SpaceX Crew Dragon spacecraft carrying NASA astronauts Robert Behnken and Douglas Hurley lifts off at 3:22 p.m. EDT on the company’s Falcon 9 rocket from NASA’s Kennedy Space Center in Florida.

*       *   May 29, 2020 – SpaceX Starship SN4 Prototype Explodes During Engine Test – A prototype using next-generation propulsion technology being developed by SpaceX for its planned heavy lift Starship rocket explodes two minutes after a brief engine test firing on the Texas Gulf Coast, destroying the test vehicle and its firing stand.

*       *   May 28, 2020 – Cessna Turboprop Completes First All-Electric Flight in Moses Lake, WA – A modified Cessna Caravan turboprop flies for the first time powered not by a gas-powered engine, but by electricity. Two Seattle-area aviation companies collaborate on the aircraft’s 30-minute, all-electric first flight at Moses Lake, Washington. MagniX designs the light electric motor, while AeroTEC modifies the airplane.

*       *   May 25, 2020 – Virgin Orbit’s LauncherOne Experiences Anomaly During Test Flight; Rocket Does Not Reach Space – Making its first flight, Virgin Orbit’s air-launched rocket fails to reach space after release from the company’s modified 747 carrier jet over the Pacific Ocean. The company’s two-stage LauncherOne suffers an anomaly soon after ignition of its first stage engine.

*       *   May 17, 2020 – ULA Atlas V Rocket Launches Carrying X-37B – At 9:14 a.m. EST, United Launch Alliance launches an Atlas 5 rocket carrying the U.S. Air Force X-37B spaceplane into orbit for its sixth mission.

*       *   May 12, 2020 – Two Xingyun Spacecraft Launch Aboard Chinese Kuaizhou 1A Rocket – A Chinese Kuaizhou 1A rocket launches carrying the first two spacecraft for China’s planned 80-satellite Xingyun communications and data relay constellation.

*       *   May 8, 2020 – Chinese Crew Module Lands Successfully – Following the launch of an uncrewed spacecraft atop a Long March 5B heavy-lift rocket on May 5, it successfuly lands in China’s desert Dongfeng landing area at 1:49 a.m., the China Aerospace Science and Technology Corp. (CASC) confirms.

*       *   May 6, 2020 – FAA Announces Remote ID Partners – The FAA announces the eight companies that will assist the federal government in establishing requirements for future suppliers of Remote Identification (Remote ID):: Airbus, AirMap, Amazon, Intel, One Sky, Skyward, T-Mobile, and Wing.

*       *   May 1, 2020 – Virgin Galactic SpaceShipTwo Conducts First Unpowered Glide Flight From Spaceport America – Virgin Galactic’s SpaceShipTwo suborbital spacecraft, called VSS Unity, conducts its first unpowered glide flight from Spaceport America.

*       *   April 29, 2020 – NASA Names Mars Helicopter “Ingenuity” – NASA officials announce that the Mars helicopter – which will launch with NASA’s Perseverance rover in the summer – will be named Ingenuity. Alabama 11th grader Vaneeza Rupani submits the name as part of NASA’s ‘Name the Rover’ contest for Mars 2020.

*       *   April 26, 2020 – SpaceX Starship SN4 Prototype Completes Key Cryogenic Pressure Test – SpaceX’s Starship SN4 survives a ‘cryo pressure test’ late Sunday night (April 26) at SpaceX’s South Texas site, setting the stage for a test flight in the near future, announces company founder and CEO Elon Musk.

*       *   April 26, 2020 – Boeing Completes Second COVID-19 Transport Mission – Boeing completes a second COVID-19 transport mission, using a Boeing Dreamlifter to bring personal protective equipment (PPE) from Hong Kong to the United States. Collaborating with Prisma Health, Atlas Air Worldwide and Discommon Founder Neil Ferrier, Boeing delivers 1.5 million medical-grade face masks for healthcare professionals at Prisma Health in South Carolina.

*       *   April 24–25, 2020 – Progress Spacecraft Launches and Docks With ISS – The Progress 75 cargo spacecraft launches atop a Russian Soyuz rocket from Baikonur Cosmodrome in Kazakhstan (April 24) at 9:51 p.m. EDT (0151 GMT and 6:51 a.m. local Baikonur time on April 25). Progress reaches the orbiting lab at 1:12 a.m. EDT (0512 GMT) on April 25 where it is expected to remain until December.

*       *   April 24, 2020 – 30th Anniversary of Launch of Hubble Space Telescope – Friday, April 24, 2020 marks the 30th anniversary of the launch of NASA’s Hubble Space Telescope.

*       *   April 21, 2020 – First Two F-35s Arrive at Alaskan Air Force Base – Two Lockheed Martin F-35 aircraft arrive at Eielson Air Force Base in Alaska to begin equipment of the 354th Fighter Wing, the first in the Pacific Air Forces theater command to receive the stealth fighter.

*       *   April 17, 2020 – Boeing Delivers Final Block II Super Hornet to USN – The Boeing Company delivers the final Block II F/A-18E/F Super Hornet to the US Navy (USN). To this point, the USN has received 322 one-seat F/A-18Es and 286 two-seat F/A-18Fs as part of the Block II Super Hornet program, launched in 2005.

*       *   April 11, 2020 – 50th Anniversary of the Launch of Apollo 13 – On this date 50 years ago, NASA’s Apollo 13 mission launches on an unexpectedly difficult journey around the moon, which becomes known as the mission that fails to reach the moon but succeeds in saving the lives of three astronauts. The seventh crewed mission in the Apollo space program, the story of Apollo 13 is captured in Ron Howard’s 1995 Academy Award-winning film “Apollo 13” starring Tom Hanks as Jim Lovell, Bill Paxton as Fred Haise, and Kevin Bacon as Jack Swigert.

*       *   April 9, 2020 – Soyuz Spacecraft Successfully Docks at ISS – A Soyuz spacecraft carrying the Expedition 63 crew launches from Baikonur Cosmodrome in Kazakhstan at 4:05 a.m. EDT and successfully docks at the International Space Station’s upper Poisk module at 10:13 a.m.

*       *   April 7, 2020 – SpaceX’s Dragon Spacecraft Returns to Earth – A Space X Dragon cargo ship returns to Earth, concluding a successful mission to the ISS while marking the last time a robotic arm will ever catch or release a SpaceX Dragon. Future SpaceX cargo missions will use an upgraded Dragon 2 spacecraft, developed for NASA’s commercial crew program, which will have 20% more cargo capacity and the ability to dock directly with the station.

*       *   March 20, 2020 – First Marine Corps F-35C Squadron Certified Safe for Flight – The first U.S. Marine Corps F-35C squadron receives a ‘safe for flight’ operations certification allowing them to train and operate independently of the Navy’s fleet replacement squadron.

*       *   March 6, 2020 – SpaceX Conducts Final Dragon Cargo Launch – SpaceX successfully conducts the final launch of an uncrewed Dragon spacecraft bringing supplies and new research to the ISS. The Falcon 9 rocket lifts off from Cape Canaveral Air Force Station at 11:50 p.m. EST. Eight minutes after liftoff, the booster touches down at SpaceX’s Landing Zone 1 facility at Cape Canaveral – marking the 50th such successful landing. The mission is SpaceX’s final flight as a part of its commercial resupply services contract with NASA. Beginning in October, resupply missions will feature SpaceX’s upgraded Dragon 2 capsule.

*       *   March 5, 2020 – NASA Announces Name of Mars 2020 Rover: Perseverance –NASA announces the name of its next Mars Rover: “Perseverance.” The name is proposed by Alex Mather, a seventh-grader from Virginia, as part of a naming contest for U.S. schoolchildren.

*       *   February 25, 2020 – Northrop Grumman’s MEV-1 Spacecraft Docks With Intelsat IS-901 Satellite Extending its Service Five More Years –Northrop Grumman’s MEV-1 spacecraft travels to and successfully docks with the Intelsat IS-901 Satellite, which is nearly 20 years old and past its anticipated lifespan, and gives it an additional five years of service, marking the first time the life of a spacecraft already in orbit is extended without human help.

*       *   February 18, 2020 – Cygnus Supply Ship Docks at ISS –Northrop Grumman’s Cygnus cargo supply ship arrives at the ISS two-and-a-half days after launch.Astronaut Drew Morgan uses the ISS’ robotic arm to capture Cygnus at 4:05 a.m. EST (0905 GMT) while the cargo freighter hovers less than 40 feet below the complex.

*       *   February 15, 2020 – Northrop Grumman Antares Rocket Launches Cygnus Supply Ship to ISS – Northrop Grumman launches the Cygnus supply ship to the ISS aboard one of its Antares rockets. Launch occurs from pad 0A at the Mid-Atlantic Regional Spaceport at 3:21:04 p.m. EST (2021:04 GMT).

*       *   February 14. 2020 – Gulfstream G700 Conducts First Flight – The Gulfstream G700 achieves first flight, departing Gulfstream Aerospace’s headquarters at Savannah/Hilton Head International Airport at 1:19 p.m., landing back at the Georgia airport two hours and 32 minutes later. The flight operates on a 30/70 blend of sustainable aviation fuel.

*       *   February 10, 2020 – Airbus Reveals Blended Wing Body Aircraft – At the Singapore Airshow, Airbus unveils MAVERIC (Model Aircraft for Validation and Experimentation of Rubust Innovative Controls), a blended wing body scale model demonstrator.

*       *   February 6, 2020 – Record-Setting NASA Astronaut Christina Koch Returns to Earth – NASA astronaut Christina Koch, who spent nearly 11 months in orbit on the longest spaceflight by a woman, lands safely in Kazakhstan along with two of her ISS crewmates – Commander Luca Parmitano of the European Space Agency and Roscosmos’ Alexander Skvortsov. In returning home, Koch concludes her 328-day mission.

*       *   January 30, 2020 – NASA Decommissions Spitzer Space Telescope – NASA decommissions the Spitzer Space Telescope after having helped unravel the mysteries of the cosmos since its launch on Aug. 25, 2003. The mission lasts much longer than expected, but with the telescope drifting away from Earth, the distance makes it too challenging to continue to operate.

*       *   January 29, 2020 – SpaceX Successfully Launches Starlink Satellites– After more than a week’s delay due to weather, SpaceX launches a Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral with 60 more satellites for the company’s Starlink Internet network.

*       *   January 25, 2020 – ISS Astronauts Successfully Repair Cosmic Ray Detector During Spacewalk – NASA’s Andrew Morgan and Italy’s Luca Parmitano conduct a spacewalk and plug a leak in a cosmic ray detector outside the ISS. As a result, the $2 billion Alpha Magnetic Spectrometer can resume its hunt for antimatter and dark matter.

*       *   January 25, 2020 – Boeing 777X Completes First Flight – The Boeing Company successfully completes its first flight of the world’s largest twin-engined jetliner, the 777X. The 777X touches down at 2:00 pm at Boeing Field outside Seattle after debuting nearly four hours earlier at Boeing’s wide-body assembly lines north of the city.

*       *   January 23, 2020 – XQ-58A Valkyrie UAV Conducts Fourth Successful Test – The US Air Force Research Laboratory (AFRL) conducts its fourth successful test of its XQ-58A Valkyrie low-cost UAV. According to the AFRL, the aircraft flies from Yuma Proving Ground in Arizona and safely lands in the desert. During the test, the XQ-58A meets all its test objectives and expands its flight envelope, including flying at an undisclosed higher altitude.

*       *   January 21, 2020 – Bell Boeing CMV-22B Osprey Conducts First Flight for US Navy – The US Navy’s Bell Boeing CMV-22B Osprey makes its initial flight at Bell’s Amarillo Assembly Center in Texas.

*       *   January 15, 2020 – Leonardo’s Falco Xplorer Conducts First Flight – Leonardo’s largest remotely piloted air system (RPAS) to date, the Falco Xplorer, makes its first flight. The Xplorer takes off from the Italian air force flight test center in Trapani, Sicily, and flies for approximately an hour over the sea before safely returning to the base.

*       *   January 13, 2020 – Airbus BelugaXL Begins Service – Airbus announces that the first BelugaXL air transport has entered service. The BelugaXL is designed to carry entire sections of Airbus aircraft from various production sites around Europe to final assembly lines.

*       *   January 11, 2020 – Iran Apologizes for Accidentally Shooting Down Plane After Days of Denying Involvement In Crash – Iran’s military announces it has accidentally shot down a Ukrainian passenger jet, blaming human error because of what it calls the plane’s unexpected turn toward a sensitive military base. Iran initially had maintained that mechanical issues had brought the Boeing aircraft down, killing all 176 people aboard.

*       *   January 10, 2020 – NASA Graduates 22nd Class of Astronauts – NASA’s 22nd class of astronauts graduate from basic training and became eligible for spaceflight assignments. The class expands NASA’s active corps to 48 members.

*       *   January 8, 2020 – China’s Ehang Tests Pilotless Air Taxi In US for The First Time – Chinese UAV manufacturer Ehang demonstrates its autonomous taxi UAV in the US for the first time, just south of Raleigh, North Carolina. The flight marks the first time Ehang receives permission to fly from the FAA, and helps pave the way for it to receive approval for passenger demonstrations in the near future.

*       *   January 7, 2020 – SpaceX Dragon Lands After ISS Resupply Mission – SpaceX’s 19th Dragon resupply mission to the International Space Station ends with the capsule’s splashdown in the Pacific Ocean southwest of Los Angeles with nearly 3,600 pounds of cargo and experiments.

*       *   January 6, 2020 – SpaceX Successfully Launches 60 More Starlink Satellites – SpaceX launches 60 more Starlink satellites – bringing the total to 180. With the launch, SpaceX continues its effort to build out a fleet of orbiting broadband relay stations that could eventually number in the thousands.

*       *   January 6, 2020 – NASA’s TESS Satellite Discovers Potentially Habitable Planet – NASA’s Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) discovers a roughly Earth-size planet in the habitable zone of its host star, the zone of orbital distances where liquid water could be stable on a world’s surface, researchers announce. The exoplanet, known as TOI 700 d, lies just 101.5 light-years from Earth.

*   January 1, 2020 – Commercial Plane Crash Deaths Drop 50% In 2019 – Dutch aviation consulting firm To70 reports that 87 accidents involving large commercial planes around the world in 2019 result in 257 fatalities, a significant reduction from the 160 accidents and 534 deaths in 2018.

2010s

#### The History of Flight from Around the World

**2010s**

* * *

**2019**

*       *   December 28, 2019 – US Astronaut Sets Record for Longest Spaceflight by a Woman – US Astronaut Christina Koch sets a record for the longest single spaceflight by a woman, breaking the old mark of 288 days with about two months remaining in her mission aboard the ISS, expected to last a total of 328 days, or nearly 11 months.

*       *   December 10, 2019 – Fully Electric Commercial Plane Completes Test Flight – A fully electric commercial plane completes a test flight in Canada in what operators call a ‘world first’ for the aviation industry. Harbour Air Seaplane CEO Greg McDougall says, “Today, we made history.” The project is conducted in conjunction with magniX, an Australian electric motor manufacturer. In a statement, magniX CEO Roei Ganzarski calls the aviation industry “ripe for disruption,” observing that the flight was both “low-cost” and “environmentally friendly.”

*       *   November 20, 2019 – Boeing 747 Factory Ends Operations – The Southern California factory that has produced fuselages for every Boeing 747 jumbo jetliner is being sold for parts, as Boeing 747-8 program supplier Triumph Group begins to clear out the storied plant with an online auction of manufacturing gear. Boeing spokesman Paul Bergman writes via email, “We are continuing to build 747-8s to meet the backlog of orders for the airplane and will continue to make the right decisions to keep the production line healthy.”

*       *   November 20, 2019 – SpaceX Starship Prototype Suffers Anomaly During Test In Texas – SpaceX’s Starship prototype ruptures during a cryogenic loading test at the company’s launch facility in South Texas. In a statement, SpaceX explains, “The purpose of today’s test was to pressurize systems to the max, so the outcome was not completely unexpected,” adding, “There were no injuries, nor is this a serious setback.”

*       *   November 5, 2019 – Untouched Apollo Moon Rock Sample Detailed – NASA unseals a pristine sample of rock and regolith at the Johnson Space Center in Houston as part of NASA’s Apollo Next-Generation Sample Analysis initiative (ANGSA). In a press release, NASA ANGSA program scientist Sarah Noble says, “We are able to make measurements today that were just not possible during the years of the Apollo program.”

*       *   November 4, 2019 – UPS Flight Forward Completes First UAV Delivery of Prescription Drugs In North Carolina – United Parcel Service Flight Forward UAVs fly prescription medications to the front lawn of a private home and to a retirement center, the UPS unit’s first-ever deliveries for drugstore chain CVS Health Corp. The packages, roughly the size of shoeboxes, are lowered from drones hovering at an altitude of about 20 feet.

*       *   October 27, 2019 – USAF X-37B Space Plane Returns After 780 Days In Orbit – The Department of Defense’s classified X-37B spaceplane lands in Florida after a record-long orbital flight lasting more than two years. The spacecraft touches down on an air strip at NASA’s Kennedy Space Center at 3:51 a.m. ET after spending 780 days orbiting Earth as the Air Force’s fifth flight mission under the Orbital Test Vehicle program.

*       *   October 24, 2019 – Gulfstream Delivers First G500 For Europe – Gulfstream Aerospace announces its first European delivery of a G500 business jet to an undisclosed customer at Gulfstream’s headquarters in Savannah, Georgia.

*       *   October 18, 2019 – NASA Conducts Historic First All-female Spacewalk – Astronauts Christina Koch and Jessica Meir successfully complete NASA’s first all-female spacewalk. The pair replace the power regulator on the International Space Station, which had been in operation since 2000.

*       *   October 18, 2019 – Wing Begins UAS Deliveries In Christiansburg, Virginia – UAV company Wing makes successful UAV deliveries to three different locations in Christiansburg, Virginia. One destination, a residential home, receives a FedEx box containing a clothing purchase from Dick’s Sporting Goods.

*       *   October 11, 2019 – Boeing, Porsche Partner On Prototype Personal Air Vehicle – The Boeing Company and Porsche sign a memorandum of understanding to study product development for the premium market for personal air vehicles.

*       *   October 4, 2019 – Oceanography Satellite Mission Ends After 11 Years – A joint U.S.-European satellite mission measuring rising sea levels for 11 years ends due to the deteriorating condition of the spacecraft’s power system. During its 11-year mission, the Jason 2 satellite outlives its design life and continues collecting precise sea level measurements through the launch of a replacement spacecraft.

*       *   October 2, 2019 – UPS Wins FAA Approval for Drone Deliveries Nationwide – UPS announces it is the first to receive approval from the FAA to operate a nationwide fleet of drones. In its announcement, UPS says it will initially set up its drone delivery program specifically for hospital campuses nationwide before expanding to other industries outside of healthcare, since the FAA has yet to write regulations to allow commercial drone flights over populated areas.

*       *   September 28, 2019 – Japanese Cargo Spacecraft Reaches ISS – JAXA’s eighth re-supply mission spacecraft reaches the International Space Station (ISS) to deliver more than four tons of crew supplies and equipment.

*       *   September 24, 2019 – JAXA, NASA Sign Lunar Exploration Agreement – JAXA and NASA sign an agreement to cooperate in U.S.-led efforts to establish a sustainable human presence at the Moon in preparation for expeditions to Mars.

*       *   September 19, 2019 – Boeing MQ-25 UAV Makes First Test Flight – A test asset for the MQ-25 unmanned tanker completes the first test flight at an airport in Illinois, the U.S. Navy and Boeing announce.

*       *   September 10, 2019 – LAPD Adds UAVs To Arsenal – UAVs become a permanent part of the Los Angeles Police Department’s crime-fighting arsenal. Regulations are approved by the five-member civilian Police Commission, authorizing the drones’ use in specific situations, including active shooters, barricaded suspects and search warrants.

*       *   September 9, 2019 – Delivery Company Unveils UAV Quadcopter – UAV delivery service provider Flirtey unveils its Eagle, a boxy quadcopter that it expects will begin delivering food and packages to households by the end of the year.

*       *   August 26, 2019 – X-37B Military Space Plane Breaks Record On Latest Military Mission – At 6:43 a.m. ET, the U.S. Air Force’s X-37B space plane surpasses its spaceflight-duration record of 717 days, 20 hours and 42 minutes, initially set during the previous mission, known as Orbital Test Vehicle 4 (OTV-4).

*       *   August 23, 2019 – NASA Activates Deep Space Atomic Clock – NASA’s Deep Space Atomic Clock (DSAC) mission team confirms that the clock has been activated successfully. The mercury-ion atomic clock, launched in June, is now in orbit around Earth and ready to begin a year-long tech demo. The DSAC, developed at NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory, is meant to be the first clock that can guide crewless spacecraft to deep space destinations.

*       *   August 22, 2019 – ULA Launches Last Ever ‘Single Stick’ Delta IV Rocket From Cape Canaveral – A United Launch Alliance Delta IV rocket, lifting off from Launch Complex 37, is the last launch featuring the “single stick” medium-lift variant.

*       *   August 21, 2019 – Astronauts Install Docking Port On ISS During Spacewalk – NASA astronauts Nick Hague and Drew Morgan conduct a spacewalk during which the duo equips the ISS with a second set of hardware to accommodate two simultaneous U.S. segment dockings.

*       *   August 14, 2019 – FAA Approves Kansas For First BVLOS UAV Flight – The Kansas Department of Transportation (KDOT) announces that the first-ever beyond-visual-line-of-sight (BVLOS) drone operation using only onboard detect-and-avoid systems in the U.S. will be conducted in Kansas following FAA approval.

*       *   July 30, 2019 – DHS Issues Cybersecurity Alert For Small Airplanes – The Department of Homeland Security (DHS) issues a security alert for small planes, warning that modern flight systems are vulnerable to hacking if someone manages to gain physical access to the aircraft.

*       *   July 24, 2019 – Apollo 11 Celebrations Culminate With Space Center Houston Party – Apollo 11 computress Mary Ellen Shirley joins a group of visitors to Space Center Houston celebrating the 50th anniversary of the Apollo 11 crew’s return to Earth. At the event, Shirley claps along with a Beatles tribute band, while those around her dance and twirl tiny American flags above their heads.

*       *   July 20, 2019 – SpaceX Cargo Ship Arrives At ISS – A SpaceX Dragon cargo ship docks with the International Space Station bringing 5,000 pounds of equipment and supplies to the outpost including a new docking mechanism that will provide a second port for commercial crew ships being built by Boeing and SpaceX.

*       *   July 20, 2019 – Soyuz MS-13 Crew Launches To ISS On Moon Landing Anniversary – NASA astronaut Drew Morgan, Roscosmos cosmonaut Aleksandr Skvortsov, and ESA astronaut Luca Parmitano launch for the International Space Station, where they plan to contribute to research supporting the return of humans to the moon in the near future. The Soyuz MS-13 crew’s liftoff occurrs 50 years to the day after Apollo 11 astronauts Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin touched down at Tranquility Base.

*       *   July 16, 2019 – Apollo 11 Astronaut Returns To Launch Pad Where First Humans Lifted Off For The Moon – Apollo 11 astronaut Michael Collins returns to the launch pad at NASA’s Kennedy Space Center in Florida where he flew to the moon 50 years ago along with the late Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin.

*       *   July 1, 2019 – NASA’s TESS Mission Finds Small Planet – NASA’s Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) discovers a tiny planet, L 98-59b, which is the smallest one it has found to date. A NASA statement says that the planet is roughly 10 percent smaller than the previously smallest planet found by TESS.

*       *   June 28, 2019 – US Navy Declares IOC For Northrop Grumman MQ-8C Fire Scout Helicopter – The U.S. Navy declares initial operational capability (IOC) for the Northrop Grumman MQ-8C Fire Scout unmanned helicopter, paving the way for the UAV to start fleet operations and training.

*       *   June 27, 2019 – First HondaJets Delivered In Hawaii To Wing Spirit – Honda Aircraft delivers its first two HondaJet Elite light jets to Wing Spirit, a newly established interisland charter operator, at an event at Daniel K. Inouye International Airport in Honolulu, Hawaii. Wing Spirit is exploring using the jets as air ambulances and for aviation education opportunities throughout the Hawaiian Islands.

*       *   June 26, 2019 – AIAA Hosts Educational Event Honoring Apollo Anniversary – NASA Armstrong Flight Research Center in California partners with the American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics, Antelope Valley chapter, to host an **Apollo 50th anniversary** event called ‘Small Steps to Giant Leaps’ at Eastside High School.

*       *   June 26, 2019 – North Dakota DOT Receives FAA Waiver To Operate UAS Over People – The North Dakota DOT receives a four-year waiver from the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) to routinely operate unmanned aircraft systems (UAS) over people. Through its waiver, the state agency will use a DJI Mavic 2 drone equipped with a ParaZero SafeAir parachute system.

*       *   June 24, 2019 – Soyuz Capsule Returns ISS Crew To Earth After 204 Days In Space – Roscosmos astronaut Oleg Kononenko, NASA astronaut Anne McClain, and Canadian Space Agency astronaut David Saint-Jacques return to Earth after spending more than 200 days on board the International Space Station. The three land aboard Russia’s Soyuz MS-11 spacecraft after touching down southeast of the town of Zhezkazgan on the steppe of Kazakhstan.

*       *   June 24, 2019 – DJI Denies Sending UAV Data To China – Chinese UAV manufacturer DJI tells a Senate subcommittee that it doesn’t collect or share any drone data, including flight logs, photos or videos, without the consent of its users.

*       *   June 22, 2019 – NASA Launches its Deep Space Atomic Clock (DSAC) – NASA launches its Deep Space Atomic Clock, or DSAC, a technology demonstration of a small, ultra-precise, mercury-ion atomic clock, placed into Earth’s orbit to test its potential as a next-generation tool for spacecraft navigation, radio science and global positioning systems.

*       *   June 20, 2019 – USAF Demonstrates New Counter-UAV Microwave Weapon – The U.S. Air Force Research Laboratory at Kirtland AFB in New Mexico demonstrates a new high-powered microwave weapon that can instantaneously down swarms of enemy drones. In the demonstration, the Tactical High Power Microwave Operational Responder, or THOR, system, disables the unmanned aerial vehicle in a flash, sending it spiraling to the ground the moment the electromagnetic ray hits it.

*       *   June 19, 2019 – Boeing Announces Relocation of Space HQ To Florida Space Coast – The Boeing Company announces it will move its space and launch business unit’s headquarters from Virginia to Titusville, along the revitalized Florida Space Coast.

*       *   June 6, 2019 – Historic Aircraft, Paratroopers Take Flight Again To Commemorate 75th Anniversary Of D-Day – As part of events to honor the military service of airborne soldiers who took part in the June 6, 1944, D-Day invasion, a fleet of C-47 transport planes drop approximately 200 parachutists over Normandy, France. Among the jumpers are World War II paratrooper veterans, and others, who jump with World War II souvenirs, some carrying items their grandfathers took into battle.

*       *   June 3, 2019 – Dragon Capsule Returns To Port With 2.1 Tons Of Cargo – SpaceX’s Dragon cargo craft returns to Earth with more than 4,200 pounds (1,900 kilograms) of equipment.

*       *   May 22, 2019 – Cosmonauts Complete Spacewalk, Honor Spacewalk Pioneer – Russian cosmonauts Oleg Kononenko and Alexey Ovchinin step outside the International Space Station where the pair send birthday greetings to former cosmonaut Alexey Leonov, the first man to walk in space, before pressing ahead with scheduled maintenance work.

*       *   May 21, 2019 – NASA Tests UAV Management System For Cities – NASA launches the final stage of a four-year effort to develop a national traffic management system for UAVs. The agency is testing beyond visual line of sight (BVLOS) flights of UAVs in cities for the first time, as businesses look to deploy the unmanned devices above busy streets and buildings.

*       *   April 23, 2019 – Mars Lander Records What’s Likely First Detected Marsquake – NASA’s robotic probe InSight has detects and measures what scientists believe to be a ‘marsquake,’ marking the first time a likely seismological tremor is recorded on another planet, the Jet Propulsion Laboratory in California reports.

*       *   April 13, 2019 – World’s Largest Plane Makes First Flight Over California – The world’s largest aircraft takes off over the Mojave Desert in California, the first flight for the carbon-composite plane built by Stratolaunch Systems Corp. Theaircraft has a wingspan the length of an American football field and is powered by six engines on a twin fuselage. The flight takes off shortly before 7 a.m. Pacific time and stays aloft for more than two hours before landing safely back at the Mojave Air and Space Port as a crowd of hundreds of people cheer.

*       *   April 11, 2019 – NASA Twins Study Shows Impact Of Time Spent In Space – Researchers publish the results of the NASA Twins Study, which find both temporary and permanent changes to astronaut Scott Kelly’s biological makeup and genetic mutations. Researchers studied the impact of time spent in space by comparing astronaut Scott Kelly, who spent 340 days in space on the ISS, with his identical twin brother, Mark Kelly.

*       *   April 9, 2019 – Alphabet’s Wing UAVs Launches Commercial Drone Delivery Service In Australia – Alphabet’s startup Wing launches its commercial drone delivery service in North Canberra, Australia. The UAVs will deliver fresh food, hot coffee and over-the-counter health products to customers’ homes.

*       *   March 29, 2019 – Gulfstream G650ER Sets Speed, Distance Jet Flight Record – Gulfstream sets a new city-pair speed record after the G650ER flies from Singapore to Tucson, Arizona, in 15 hours and 23 minutes, eclipsing the previous record by 44 minutes, set by a Bombardier Global 7500. The flight also establishes a new record for the farthest business jet flight in history.

*       *   March 29, 2019 –Japan Declares F-35A Squadron Initial-Operation-Capable – Japan’s air force stands up its first operational F-35A fighter squadron, the 302nd Tactical Fighter Squadron. The nation joins the US and other military services with operational F-35 squadrons, marking the first F-35 IOC for an Indo-Pacific region customer.

*       *   March 26, 2019 – VP Pence Says US Is In A New Space Race With China And Russia – In remarks during the fifth meeting of the National Space Council (NSC) at the U.S. Space & Rocket Center in Huntsville, Alabama, Vice President Mike Pence says, “Make no mistake about it – we’re in a space race today, just as we were in the 1960s, and the stakes are even higher.” Adds Pence, “The United States must remain first in space in this century as in the last, not just to propel our economy and secure our nation but, above all, because the rules and values of space, like every great frontier, will be written by those who have the courage to get there first and the commitment to stay.”

*       *   March 24, 2019 –Bombardier’s Global 7500 Aircraft Breaks Key Speed Record Between Los Angeles and New York – Bombardier’s Global 7500 business jet departs Van Nuys Airport at 7:01 AM local time, and arrives at Teterboro Airport at 1:55 PM local time, completing the coast-to-coast mission in 3 hours and 54 minutes, beating the previously held record established by a competing aircraft manufacturer and making it the fastest business jet ever to complete this route.

*       *   March 19, 2019 – OSIRIS-REx Finds Surprises At Bennu Asteroid – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft, flying in close formation with a tiny asteroid named Bennu, finds clear signs of water-rich clays on its rock-strewn surface, as well as another mineral – magnetite – that forms in aqueous environments, project scientists announce.

*       *   March 18, 2019 – NASA Announces It Wants To Put A Rover On The Moon By 2023 – NASA officials announce at a NASA town hall during the Lunar and Planetary Science Conference that the agency seeks to land a rover on the moon by 2023.

*       *   March 12, 2019 – Toyota, JAXA Partner To Develop Moon Rover – Japan’s space agency announces that they have agreed to cooperate with Toyota to develop a crewed lunar rover that runs on fuel cell technologies.

*       *   March 8, 2019 – SpaceX’s Crew Dragon Arrives At Port Canaveral – SpaceX’s Crew Dragon splashes down in the Atlantic Ocean around 8:45 am Eastern time about 250 miles east of Kennedy Space Center in Florida, roughly six hours after undocking.

*       *   March 7, 2019 – USAF Completes First Flight Test Of XQ-58A Valkyrie Demonstrator – The USAF completes the first flight test of the long-range, high subsonic unmanned air vehicle, XQ-58A Valkyrie demonstrator at Yuma Proving Grounds in Arizona.

*       *   February 27, 2019 – US Navy Declares Lockheed Martin F-35C Has Achieved IOC – The US Navy announces that the Lockheed Martin F-35C Lightning II has achieved Initial Operational Capability (IOC). The Navy’s first F-35C squadron completes aircraft carrier qualifications aboard the USS Carl Vinson and receives a Safe-For-Flight Operations Certification.

*       *   February 27, 2019 – Boeing Unveils Unmanned Combat Jet Developed In Australia – The Boeing Company unveils a prototype fighter-like jet developed in Australia and designed to fly alongside crewed aircraft in combat for a fraction of the cost. The multi-role, uncrewed aircraft is Australia’s first domestically developed combat aircraft since World War II.

*       *   February 19, 2019 – NASA Selects Organizations In Texas, Nevada To Test UAV Traffic Management – NASA chooses two organizations to host the last stage of technical demonstrations involving drones: the Lone Star UAS Center for Excellence & Innovation in Corpus Christi, Texas; and the Nevada Institute for Autonomous Systems in Las Vegas. The two locations will host demonstrations to confirm that NASA’s UAS Traffic Management system can safely and effectively manage drone traffic in an urban area.

*       *   February 14, 2019 – Airbus Shuts Down A380 – Airbus announces it will be abandoning the production of the A380 superjumbo due to lackluster sales, with the last A380 scheduled to be delivered in 2021.

*       *   February 11, 2019 – Virgin Galactic Donates SpaceShipTwo Rocket Motor To Smithsonian – Virgin Galactic announces that the SpaceShipTwo rocket motor has been donated to the Smithsonian Institution’s National Air and Space Museum in Washington, DC, and will be exhibited in its Future of Spaceflight gallery, due to open in 2024.

*       *   February 5, 2019 – Delta Celebrates First Flights Of P&W-Powered Airbus A220 – The first flights of Delta’s first Airbus A220 jets take off from New York’s LaGuardia Airport with one flight to Boston and another to Dallas/Fort Worth International Airport. Equipped with new Pratt and Whitney geared turbofan engines, the A220s have higher fuel efficiency compared with other models.

*       *   February 5, 2019 – Boeing Announces Investment In Aerion’s Supersonic Business Jets – The Boeing Company announces that it has made a sizable investment in supersonic business jet developer Aerion. Boeing plans to provide engineering, manufacturing, and flight testing services for Aerion’s $120 million supersonic business jet, slated for first flight in 2023.

*       *   January 25, 2019 – US Air Force Receives First Two Boeing KC-46 Tankers – The US Air Force’s first two Boeing KC-46 tankers touch down at McConnell Air Force base in Kansas after departing the company’s Everett, Washington, facility.

*       *   January 25, 2019 – Blue Origin Opens Alabama Rocket Engine Plant – Blue Origin celebrates the groundbreaking of a new rocket engine production facility in Huntsville, Alabama. The plant will produce Blue Origin’s BE-4 engine.

*       *   January 24, 2019 – ISRO Successfully Launches Military Satellite In First Mission of 2019 – India launches its first rocket of 2019, lifting two payloads into orbit: a military imaging satellite and a student-built orbital laboratory. The ISRO Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle (PSLV) launches from India’s Satish Dhawan Space Centre.

*       *   January 22, 2019 – Boeing’s Self-Flying Taxi Prototype Completes First Test Flight – The Boeing Company’s flying car prototype hovers briefly in the air during an inaugural test flight. The 30-foot-long hybrid aircraft, part helicopter, part drone, and part fixed-wing plane, lifts a few feet off the ground and makes a soft landing after less than a minute of being airborne.

*       *   January 16, 2019 – F-22 Receives First Metal 3D-Printed Component – A metallic 3D-printed component is installed on an operational F-22 Raptor for the first time, which the Air Force hopes will lower costs and reduce maintenance time.

*       *   January 14, 2019 – China Exchanges Data On Lunar Landing With NASA – China exchanges data with NASA regarding its recent mission to land a Chinese spacecraft on the far side of the moon, the Chinese space agency says, in what is the first such collaboration since an American law banned joint space projects with China that do not have prior congressional approval.

*       *   January 13, 2019 – Boeing 727 Makes Final Commercial Flight– The final Boeing 727 commercial aircraft to carry passengers makes its last flight. The flight, operated by Iran Aseman Airlines, is a two-hour domestic route from Zahedan to Tehran’s Mehrabad International Airport.

*       *   January 10, 2019 – SpaceX Reveals Starship Test Rocket– SpaceX founder and CEO Elon Musk announces that assembly of the test-flight version of SpaceX’s Mars-colonizing Starship vehicle is complete, and posts a photo of the vehicle to Twitter.

*       *   January 2, 2019 – New Horizons Transmits Images Of Ultima Thule – NASA’s New Horizons spacecraft – 4 billion miles from Earth – yields its first close-up pictures of the most distant celestial object ever explored: the icy space object Ultima Thule.

* * *

**2018**

*       *   December 21, 2018 – Rockets Launch At Highest Rate In Two Decades In 2018 – Across the globe, more than 100 orbital launches take place in 2018, the highest rate in two decades.

*       *   December 18, 2018 – Airbus Helicopters Successfully Completes H160’s First Flight – Airbus Helicopters successfully carries out the first flight of the initial serial production H160 twin-engine medium helicopter, as the company prepares for European Aviation Safety Agency (EASA) type certification in late 2019.

*       *   December 17, 2018 – Gulfstream G650ER Sets World Speed Record – Gulfstream Aerospace sets a world speed record in a G650ER on a flight from Teterboro, New Jersey, to Dubai, a distance of 6,142 nm in 11 hr., 2 min. at an average speed of Mach 0.90, beating the previous record by one hour, 48 minutes.

*       *   December 12, 2018 – China’s Chang’e-4 Spacecraft Successfully Enters Lunar Orbit – China’s Chang’e-4 lander and rover spacecraft successfully enters lunar orbit following a four-and-half-day flight to the moon.

*       *   December 10, 2018 – NASA’s Voyager 2 Probe Enters Interstellar Space – NASA’s Voyager 2 probe, launched in 1977 and designed for just a five-year mission, becomes only the second human-made object to enter interstellar space as it continues its marathon trek billions of miles from Earth.

*       *   December 3, 2018 – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx Spacecraft Reaches Bennu Asteroid – NASA’s OSIRIS-REx spacecraft reaches the Bennu asteroid after a two-year chase, pulling within 12 miles of the space rock.

*       *   December 3, 2018 – Russian Soyuz Spacecraft Docks With ISS – A Russian Soyuz FG rocket successfully launches into orbit carrying three crew members on a six-hour flight to the International Space Station. The spacecraft docks at the stations’s upper Poisk module at 12:33 p.m. EDT.

*       *   November 28, 2018 – US, UK Reach Post-Brexit Open Skies Agreement – The US and Britain reach an agreement to maintain air service between the two countries after Brexit.

*       *   November 26, 2018 – NASA’s InSight Lander Set Solar Power Record – NASA’s InSight lander touches down on Mars and hours later successfully extends its large solar arrays thus setting a record for generating more electrical power in one day than any previous Mars vehicle has during its first day on the red planet.

*       *   November 26, 2018 – Airbus Delivers First A330neo – Airbus delivers its first A330neo jet to TAP Portugal.

*       *   November 19, 2018 – Musk Announces Renaming of BFR to Starship, Super Heavy – SpaceX CEO Elon Musk announces that SpaceX’s next-generation launch system, previously known as the Big Falcon Rocket (BFR) has a new name: Starship and Super Heavy.

*       *   November 19, 2018 – NASA Announces Landing Site For Mars 2020 Rover – NASA announces that its next Mars rover will search the Jezero Crater and delta for the chemical building blocks of life and other signs of past microbes. The rover is scheduled to launch in July 2020.

*       *   November 18, 2018 – Virgin Orbit Completes First Captive Carry Flight – Lockheed Martin’s experimental X-59 Quiet Supersonic Technology aircraft, designed to cruise at 55,000 feet and reach speeds of about 940 miles per hour, officially enters production, as part of a $250 million NASA contract.

*       *   November 16, 2018 – X-59 Supersonic Jet Enters Production – Virgin Orbit successfully performs the first captive carry flight of its LauncherOne air-launch system, moving the company closer to first flight.

*       *   November 10, 2018 – JAXA HSRC Survives Re-Entry – JAXA’s HTV Small Re-Entry Capsule (HSRC) becomes the first Japanese spacecraft to bring experiments back to Earth from the space station. The HSRC is released from the Japanese HTV-7 cargo resupply vessel during reentry following a deorbit burn before beginning its own descent. The capsule is recovered and brought to JAXA headquarters at the Tsukuba Space Center for analysis.

*       *   October 29, 2018 – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe Sets Record For Closest Approach To Sun – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe moves closer to the sun than any spacecraft before, passing the previous record of 26.6 million miles (43 million kilometers) set by Helios-2 back in 1976, and is expected to keep approaching until it flies through the corona, or outer atmosphere, passing within 15 million miles (24 million kilometers) of the solar surface.

*       *   October 18, 2018 – US Navy Commissions First UAV Test Squadron – The US Navy formally commissions its first test squadron dedicated to UAVs

*       *   October 18, 2018 – US Air Force’s X-37B Space Plane Passes 400 Days In Orbit – The US Air Force’s X-37B space plane passes the 400-day mark for its classified mission in orbit. The Orbital Test Vehicle (OTV-5) mission begins October 7, 2017 when it launches mounted atop a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket from Launch Complex 39A at NASA’s Kennedy Space Center in Florida.

*       *   October 14, 2018 – Second NASA Space Telescope Enters Safe Mode – A second NASA space telescope shuts down and halts science observations. Less than a week after the Hubble Space Telescope goes offline, the Chandra X-ray Observatory experiences a similar issue.

*       *   October 11, 2018 – Pentagon Temporarily Grounds F-35 – The Pentagon orders a temporary pause in all F-35 fighter jet flights in order to inspect the fleet in the wake of a September crash in South Carolina. The issue revolves around a potentially bad fuel tube, and affects more than 250 U.S.-owned jets, as well as nearly 100 that belong to other nations including Britain.

*       *   October 10, 2018 – US Navy Begins Formal F-35C Testing – The US Navy begins to formally test the operation of an F-35C stealth fighter intended to demonstrate fulfillment of the service’s requirements.

*       *   October 9, 2018 – Insitu Debuts UAV With “ID-Capable” Camera – Insitu unveils a new UAV, the Alticam-14, which has video imaging capability high enough in quality to positively identify people from the air.

*       *   October 5, 2018 – Trump Signs FAA Reauthorization Bill – President Donald Trump signs the FAA Reauthorization Act of 2018, extending FAA funding through 2023 just ahead of an October 7 deadline.

*       *   October 5, 2018 – Hubble Space Telescope Disabled After Gyroscope Fails – The Hubble Space Telescope, NASA’s jewel of the skies, goes temporarily offline. The telescope enters into a safe mode after one of its gyroscopes fails. NASA appoints a review board to investigate the gyroscope problem.

*       *   October 3, 2018 – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe Successfully Passes Venus – NASA’s Parker Solar Probe passes within 1,500 miles of Venus on its way to study the sun.
